  "short_description": {
    "id": "Changeling (juga auf atau oaf) adalah makhluk mirip manusia dalam cerita rakyat Eropa: pengganti yang ditinggalkan makhluk gaib ketika menculik manusia, biasanya bayi.",
    "en": "A changeling (also auf or oaf) is a human-like creature of European folklore: a substitute left by a supernatural being when it kidnapped a human, usually a baby.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "changeling-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Changeling, dulu juga disebut auf atau oaf, adalah makhluk mirip manusia yang dikenal di sebagian besar cerita rakyat Eropa (European folklore). Menurut cerita rakyat, changeling adalah pengganti yang ditinggalkan makhluk gaib ketika menculik manusia; kadang berupa sepotong kayu yang disihir menyerupai korbannya, tetapi lebih sering makhluk gaib yang disihir agar tampak seperti manusia yang diculik. Pelaku penculikan bisa peri, iblis, troll, nereid, dan makhluk lain; korbannya biasanya anak-anak, tetapi di Skandinavia dan Irlandia orang dewasa juga diambil.",
      "en": "Known in older English as an auf or oaf, the changeling is a human-looking being that turns up across most of Europe's folk traditions. According to folklore it was a substitute left by a supernatural being when it kidnapped a human; sometimes a piece of wood magically made to resemble the victim, but more often a supernatural being magically made to look like the stolen person. The kidnappers could be fairies, demons, trolls, nereids and others; victims were usually children, but in Scandinavia and Ireland adults were taken too.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "changeling-c01",
        "changeling-c02",
        "changeling-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Dalam legenda Irlandia, anak peri bisa tampak sakit-sakitan dan tidak tumbuh seperti anak biasa, berjanggut atau bergigi panjang, dan menunjukkan kecerdasan jauh di atas usianya. Jimat sederhana seperti mantel yang dibalik atau gunting besi terbuka di dekat tempat tidur anak dipercaya menangkalnya. Sebagian sarjana modern berpendapat kisah anak yang ditukar berasal dari penjelasan rakyat atas autisme atau kondisi perkembangan lain; kasus Bridget Cleary, yang dibunuh suaminya karena dianggap changeling, adalah salah satu yang paling terkenal.",
      "en": "In Irish legend a fairy child may look sickly and fail to grow like a normal child, have a beard or long teeth, and show intelligence far beyond its years. People guarded a sleeping child with simple charms, for instance a coat turned inside out or a pair of open iron scissors. Some modern scholars argue that tales of swapped children began as folk explanations of autism or other developmental conditions; the case of Bridget Cleary, killed by her husband who thought her a changeling, is among the best known.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "changeling-c04",
        "changeling-c05",
        "changeling-c06",
        "changeling-c07"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Menurut artikel de, dalam takhayul Eropa abad pertengahan Wechselbalg adalah bayi yang ditukarkan makhluk iblis dengan bayi seorang ibu baru untuk mengganggu dan merugikan manusia; dalam kepercayaan non-Kristen ia dilahirkan Druden, kurcaci, atau elf, sedangkan dalam kepercayaan Kristen ia anak penyihir atau bahkan Iblis, yang terutama mengincar bayi belum dibaptis. Istilah Wechselbalg pertama muncul awal abad ke-11.",
      "en": "According to the de article, in medieval European superstition the Wechselbalg was an infant swapped by a demonic being for a new mother's own baby, to harass and harm people; in non-Christian belief it was fathered by Druden, dwarfs or elves, while in Christian belief it was the child of witches or even the Devil, who especially sought unbaptized babies. The term Wechselbalg first appears in the early 11th century.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "changeling-c08",
        "changeling-c09",
        "changeling-c10",
        "changeling-c11"
      ]
    }
  ],

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "changeling-c01",
      "source_id": "changeling-s1",
      "quote": "A changeling, also historically referred to as an auf or oaf, is a human-like creature found throughout much of European folklore. According to folklore, a changeling was a substitute left by a supernatural being when kidnapping a human being.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Changeling (auf atau oaf) adalah makhluk mirip manusia dalam cerita rakyat Eropa (European folklore), pengganti yang ditinggalkan makhluk gaib saat menculik manusia. Dulu disebut dalam bahasa Inggris (English) dan dikenal di Eropa (Europe).",
        "en": "A changeling (auf or oaf) is a human-like creature of European folklore, a substitute left by a supernatural being when kidnapping a human."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "changeling-c02",
      "source_id": "changeling-s1",
      "quote": "Although sometimes it was a \"stock\" (a piece of wood made magically to resemble the kidnapped human), more often the changeling was a supernatural being made magically to look like the kidnapped human.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kadang changeling sepotong kayu (stock) yang disihir, lebih sering makhluk gaib yang disihir menyerupai korban.",
        "en": "Sometimes the changeling was a stock of wood, more often a supernatural being magically made to look like the victim."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "changeling-c03",
      "source_id": "changeling-s1",
      "quote": "Supernatural beings blamed for stealing children included fairies, demons, trolls, nereids and many others. Usually, the kidnapped human was a child; but there were cases, particularly in Scandinavia and Ireland, where adults were taken.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Penculiknya peri, iblis, troll, nereid; korbannya biasanya anak, tetapi di Skandinavia (Scandinavia) dan Irlandia (Ireland) orang dewasa juga.",
        "en": "Kidnappers included fairies, demons, trolls and nereids; victims were usually children, but adults too in Scandinavia and Ireland."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "changeling-c04",
      "source_id": "changeling-s1",
      "quote": "In Irish legend, a fairy child may appear sickly and will not grow in size like a normal child, and may have notable physical characteristics such as a beard or long teeth. They may also display intelligence far beyond their apparent years and possess uncanny insight.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam legenda Irlandia (Irish), anak peri tampak sakit, tak tumbuh, berjanggut atau bergigi panjang, dan cerdas melampaui usianya.",
        "en": "In Irish legend a fairy child looks sickly, does not grow, may have a beard or long teeth, and is clever beyond its years."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "changeling-c05",
      "source_id": "changeling-s1",
      "quote": "Simple charms such as an inverted coat or open iron scissors left where the child sleeps were thought to ward them off",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mantel terbalik atau gunting besi terbuka di dekat anak tidur dipercaya menangkalnya.",
        "en": "An inverted coat or open iron scissors where the child sleeps were thought to ward them off."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "changeling-c06",
      "source_id": "changeling-s1",
      "quote": "Some modern scholars have argued these stories of replaced children originated as folklore explanations for autism or other developmental conditions.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebagian sarjana modern berpendapat kisah ini berasal dari penjelasan rakyat atas autisme atau kondisi perkembangan lain.",
        "en": "Some modern scholars argue these stories began as folk explanations for autism or other developmental conditions."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "changeling-c07",
      "source_id": "changeling-s1",
      "quote": "Bridget Cleary is one of the most well-known cases of an adult thought to be a changeling by her family: her husband killed her, attempting to force the fairies to return his 'real' wife.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Bridget Cleary, dewasa yang dianggap changeling, dibunuh suaminya yang ingin memaksa peri mengembalikan istrinya yang asli.",
        "en": "Bridget Cleary, an adult thought a changeling, was killed by her husband trying to force the fairies to return his real wife."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "changeling-c08",
      "source_id": "changeling-s2",
      "quote": "Der Wechselbalg war im Aberglauben des europäischen Mittelalters ein Säugling (veraltet „Balg“), der einer Wöchnerin durch ein dämonisches Wesen im Austausch gegen ihr eigenes Kind mit der Absicht untergeschoben wurde, die Menschen zu belästigen und ihnen zu schaden.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam takhayul (Aberglauben) Eropa abad pertengahan, Wechselbalg adalah bayi (Säugling) yang ditukar makhluk iblis (dämonisches Wesen) untuk mengganggu dan merugikan manusia (Menschen).",
        "en": "In medieval European superstition (Aberglauben) the Wechselbalg was an infant (Säugling) swapped by a demonic being (dämonisches Wesen) to harass and harm people (Menschen)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "changeling-c09",
      "source_id": "changeling-s2",
      "quote": "In nichtchristlichen Glaubensvorstellungen wurde der Wechselbalg von Druden, Zwergen oder Elfen gezeugt und untergeschoben.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam kepercayaan non-Kristen (nichtchristlichen), Wechselbalg dilahirkan Druden, kurcaci (Zwergen), atau elf (Elfen).",
        "en": "In non-Christian (nichtchristlichen) belief the Wechselbalg was fathered by Druden, dwarfs (Zwergen) or elves (Elfen)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "changeling-c10",
      "source_id": "changeling-s2",
      "quote": "Der Wechselbalg im christlichen Volksglauben war ein Kind von Hexen oder sogar des Teufels. Der Teufel interessierte sich besonders für ungetaufte Kinder",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam kepercayaan Kristen (christlichen), Wechselbalg anak penyihir (Hexen) atau Iblis (Teufels), yang mengincar anak belum dibaptis (ungetaufte Kinder).",
        "en": "In Christian (christlichen) belief the Wechselbalg was a child of witches (Hexen) or the Devil (Teufels), who sought unbaptized children (ungetaufte Kinder)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "changeling-c11",
      "source_id": "changeling-s2",
      "quote": "Der für das Böse und Unheimliche stehende Begriff Wechselbalg taucht Anfang des 11. Jahrhunderts erstmals auf.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Istilah (Begriff) Wechselbalg pertama muncul awal abad ke-11 (11. Jahrhunderts).",
        "en": "The term (Begriff) Wechselbalg first appears in the early 11th century (11. Jahrhunderts)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "changeling-c12",
      "source_id": "changeling-s2",
      "quote": "Die verängstigte Mutter betrachtete wiederum die Taufe als den besten Schutz vor einem Wechselbalg.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ibu (Mutter) yang takut menganggap baptisan (Taufe) pelindung terbaik (besten Schutz) dari Wechselbalg.",
        "en": "The frightened mother (Mutter) saw baptism (Taufe) as the best protection (besten Schutz) from a Wechselbalg."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Taranis adalah dewa guntur Kelt yang tercatat dalam sumber sastra dan prasasti, disamakan orang Romawi dengan Jupiter.",
    "en": "Taranis is a Celtic thunder god known from literary and inscriptional sources, equated by the Romans with Jupiter.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "taranis-c01",
      "taranis-c03"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Taranis (kadang Taranus atau Tanarus) adalah dewa guntur Kelt (Celtic) yang tercatat dalam sumber sastra dan prasasti. Epos Pharsalia karya penyair Romawi Lucan menyebut Taranis, Esus, dan Teutates sebagai dewa yang diberi kurban manusia oleh orang Galia. Scholia atas puisi Lucan menyebut korban Taranis dibakar dalam wadah kayu berongga, yang dibandingkan dengan wicker man yang digambarkan Caesar. Menurut artikel de, Taranis adalah dewa langit, cuaca, dan guntur, yang menurut Lucan berada di puncak dunia dewa Kelt bersama Teutates dan Esus.",
      "en": "Taranis (sometimes Taranus or Tanarus) is a Celtic thunder god known from literary and epigraphic sources. The Roman poet Lucan's epic Pharsalia names Taranis, Esus and Teutates as gods to whom the Gauls offered human sacrifice. The scholia on Lucan's poem say the victims of Taranis were burned in a hollow wooden container, a rite compared with the wicker man described by Caesar. According to the de article Taranis is a god of sky, weather and thunder who, in Lucan, stood with Teutates and Esus at the head of the Celtic pantheon.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "taranis-c01",
        "taranis-c02",
        "taranis-c07"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Scholia yang sama menyebut orang Romawi mungkin menyamakannya dengan Dis Pater atau Jupiter; para sarjana lebih menyukai Jupiter, karena Taranis juga disamakan dengan Jupiter dalam prasasti, dan sebagian mengaitkannya dengan \"dewa roda\" Kelt yang digambarkan membawa roda berjari-jari dan petir. Prasasti pemujaannya berasal dari abad ke-4 SM hingga abad ke-3 M. Namanya berasal dari kata Proto-Kelt *torano- (\"guntur\"), sekerabat dengan torann dalam bahasa Irlandia Kuno dan taran dalam bahasa Wales; sebuah altar di Chester (154 M) dan prasasti di Dalmatia menyebutnya Jupiter Tanaris. Namanya juga sepadan secara etimologis dengan dewa Jermanik Donar (Thor).",
      "en": "The same scholia say the Romans may have equated him with Dis Pater or with Jupiter; scholars favour Jupiter, since inscriptions also pair Taranis with Jupiter, and some link him to the Celtic \"wheel god\" shown with a spoked wheel and a thunderbolt. Inscriptions to him run from the 4th century BCE to the 3rd century CE. His name comes from Proto-Celtic *torano- (\"thunder\"), akin to Old Irish torann and Welsh taran; an altar at Chester (154 AD) and an inscription in Dalmatia call him Jupiter Tanaris. His name also matches that of the Germanic god Donar (Thor) etymologically.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "taranis-c03",
        "taranis-c04",
        "taranis-c05",
        "taranis-c06",
        "taranis-c08",
        "taranis-c09"
      ]
    }
  ],

  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "taranis-c01",
      "source_id": "taranis-s1",
      "quote": "Taranis (sometimes Taranus or Tanarus) is a Celtic thunder god attested in literary and epigraphic sources. The Roman poet Lucan's epic Pharsalia mentions Taranis, Esus, and Teutates as gods to whom the Gauls sacrificed humans.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Taranis (Taranus, Tanarus) adalah dewa guntur Kelt (Celtic); Pharsalia karya Lucan (Roman) menyebut Taranis, Esus, dan Teutates sebagai dewa yang diberi kurban manusia oleh orang Galia (Gauls).",
        "en": "Taranis (Taranus, Tanarus) is a Celtic thunder god; the Roman poet Lucan's Pharsalia names Taranis, Esus and Teutates as gods given human sacrifice by the Gauls."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "taranis-c02",
      "source_id": "taranis-s1",
      "quote": "in particular, that the victims of Taranis were burned in a hollow wooden container. This sacrifice has been compared with the wicker man described by Caesar.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Korban Taranis dibakar dalam wadah kayu berongga, dibandingkan dengan wicker man yang digambarkan Caesar.",
        "en": "Victims of Taranis were burned in a hollow wooden container, compared with the wicker man described by Caesar."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "taranis-c03",
      "source_id": "taranis-s1",
      "quote": "These scholia also tell us that Taranis was perhaps either equated by the Romans with Dis Pater, Roman god of the underworld, or Jupiter, Roman god of weather. Scholars have preferred the latter equation to the former, as Taranis is also equated with Jupiter in inscriptions.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Scholia (scholia) menyebut orang Romawi (Romans) menyamakan Taranis dengan Dis Pater atau Jupiter; sarjana lebih menyukai Jupiter karena prasasti juga demikian.",
        "en": "The scholia say the Romans equated Taranis with Dis Pater or Jupiter; scholars prefer Jupiter, as inscriptions agree."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "taranis-c04",
      "source_id": "taranis-s1",
      "quote": "This god, known only from iconographic sources, is depicted with a spoked wheel and the attributes of Jupiter (including a thunderbolt).",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dewa roda (wheel god) Kelt digambarkan dengan roda berjari-jari dan atribut Jupiter, termasuk petir (thunderbolt).",
        "en": "The Celtic wheel god is shown with a spoked wheel and Jupiter's attributes, including a thunderbolt."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "taranis-c05",
      "source_id": "taranis-s1",
      "quote": "Various inscriptions attest to Taranis's worship, dating between the 4th century BCE and the 3rd century CE.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Prasasti pemujaan Taranis berasal dari abad ke-4 SM hingga abad ke-3 M.",
        "en": "Inscriptions to Taranis date from the 4th century BCE to the 3rd century CE."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "taranis-c06",
      "source_id": "taranis-s1",
      "quote": "The issue was settled by the discovery of a dedication to Iovi Tanaro ('Jupiter Tanaris') in Dalmatia, which confirms that this form did exist.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Persembahan kepada Iovi Tanaro (Jupiter Tanaris) di Dalmatia (Dalmatia) membuktikan bentuk Tanaris; altar Chester (154 M) juga.",
        "en": "A dedication to Iovi Tanaro (Jupiter Tanaris) in Dalmatia confirms the form Tanaris; so does the Chester altar (154 AD)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "taranis-c07",
      "source_id": "taranis-s2",
      "quote": "Taranis ist ein keltischer Gott des Himmels, des Wetters und des Donners. Dem römischen Dichter Lucan zufolge stand er mit Teutates und Esus an der Spitze der keltischen Götterwelt.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Taranis adalah dewa Kelt (keltischer Gott) langit (Himmels), cuaca (Wetters), dan guntur (Donners); menurut Lucan ia di puncak dunia dewa Kelt bersama Teutates dan Esus.",
        "en": "Taranis is a Celtic god (keltischer Gott) of sky (Himmels), weather (Wetters) and thunder (Donners); in Lucan he stood with Teutates and Esus atop the Celtic pantheon."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "taranis-c08",
      "source_id": "taranis-s1",
      "quote": "The name Taranis derives from proto-Celtic *torano- (\"thunder\"), which in turn derives from the proto-Indo-European root *(s)tenh₂- (\"to thunder\"). Through this proto-Celtic etymon, the theonym is cognate with words for thunder in Old Irish (torann ), Old Breton (taran ), Middle Welsh (taran )",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nama Taranis dari Proto-Kelt (proto-Celtic) *torano- (guntur, thunder), sekerabat dengan torann (Old Irish, Irlandia Kuno) dan taran (Middle Welsh, Wales).",
        "en": "The name Taranis comes from proto-Celtic *torano- (thunder), cognate with Old Irish torann and Middle Welsh taran."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "taranis-c09",
      "source_id": "taranis-s1",
      "quote": "Taranis's name corresponds etymologically to that of the Germanic god Donar (i.e., Thor).",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nama Taranis sepadan secara etimologis dengan dewa Jermanik (Germanic) Donar (Thor).",
        "en": "Taranis's name corresponds etymologically to the Germanic god Donar (Thor)."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],
  "gaps": [
    {
      "field": "images",
      "searched": "Tidak ada gambar dengan lisensi yang diperiksa."
    },
    {
      "field": "sources",

    },
    {
      "field": "images",
      "searched": "Gambar Commons belum dicari pada putaran ini; dijadwalkan pada pemeriksaan gambar terpisah."
    }
  ]
}

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Brigid (Brigit, Bríg) adalah dewi Irlandia pra-Kristen dari Tuatha Dé Danann, putri Dagda, yang dikaitkan dengan kebijaksanaan, puisi, penyembuhan, perlindungan, dan pandai besi.",
    "en": "Brigid (Brigit, Bríg) is a goddess of pre-Christian Ireland from the Tuatha Dé Danann, daughter of the Dagda, associated with wisdom, poetry, healing, protection and smithing.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "brigid-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Brigid atau Brigit (\"yang ditinggikan\"), juga Bríg, adalah dewi Irlandia pra-Kristen. Dalam mitologi Irlandia ia anggota Tuatha Dé Danann, putri Dagda dan istri Bres, dengan putra bernama Ruadán. Ia dikaitkan dengan kebijaksanaan, puisi, penyembuhan, perlindungan, pandai besi, dan hewan ternak. Cormac's Glossary dari abad ke-9 menyebutnya \"dewi yang dipuja para penyair\" dan menyebut dua saudarinya, Brigid sang penyembuh dan Brigid sang pandai besi, sehingga ia mungkin dewi tiga rupa.",
      "en": "Brigid or Brigit (\"exalted one\"), also Bríg, is a goddess of pre-Christian Ireland. In Irish mythology she is one of the Tuatha Dé Danann, daughter of the Dagda and wife of Bres, with a son named Ruadán. She is tied to wisdom, poetry, healing, protection, smithing and domestic animals. Cormac's Glossary of the 9th century calls her \"the goddess whom poets adored\" and names two sisters, Brigid the healer and Brigid the smith, suggesting a triple deity.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "brigid-c01",
        "brigid-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Santa Brigid memiliki banyak atribut sang dewi, dan hari rayanya, 1 Februari, semula adalah perayaan Imbolc; karena itu ada yang berpendapat sang santa adalah kristenisasi sang dewi. Namanya berasal dari kata Proto-Kelt *Brigantī (\"yang tinggi\"), sekerabat dengan nama dewi Britania kuno Brigantia. Menurut artikel de, tempat sucinya di Kildare, tempat api sucinya dijaga; dalam bahasa Wales namanya Ffraid, dan ia dipuja dalam tiga wujud sebagai pelindung penyair, pembuat hukum, penyembuhan, kesuburan, dan pandai besi.",
      "en": "Much of the goddess lives on in Saint Brigid, whose 1 February feast began as the festival of Imbolc; some therefore see the saint as the goddess in Christian guise. Her name comes from Proto-Celtic *Brigantī (\"the high one\"), cognate with the ancient British goddess Brigantia. According to the de article her sanctuary was at Kildare, where her sacred fire was kept; her Welsh name is Ffraid, and she was worshipped in threefold form as patron of poets, lawgivers, healing, fertility and smiths.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "brigid-c03",
        "brigid-c04",
        "brigid-c06",
        "brigid-c07"
      ]
    }

    {
      "id": "brigid-c01",
      "source_id": "brigid-s1",
      "quote": "is a goddess of pre-Christian Ireland. She appears in Irish mythology as a member of the Tuatha Dé Danann, the daughter of the Dagda and wife of Bres, with whom she had a son named Ruadán. She is associated with wisdom, poetry, healing, protection, smithing and domesticated animals.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Brigid adalah dewi Irlandia (Ireland) pra-Kristen, anggota Tuatha Dé Danann, putri Dagda, istri Bres, ibu Ruadán, terkait kebijaksanaan, puisi, penyembuhan, perlindungan, dan pandai besi.",
        "en": "Brigid is a goddess of pre-Christian Ireland, one of the Tuatha Dé Danann, daughter of the Dagda, wife of Bres, mother of Ruadán, tied to wisdom, poetry, healing, protection and smithing."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "brigid-c02",
      "source_id": "brigid-s1",
      "quote": "Cormac's Glossary, written in the 9th century by Christian monks, says that Brigid was \"the goddess whom poets adored\" and that she had two sisters: Brigid the healer and Brigid the smith. This suggests she may have been a triple deity.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Cormac's Glossary abad ke-9 menyebut Brigid dewi pujaan penyair dan saudarinya Brigid penyembuh dan Brigid pandai besi; mungkin dewi tiga rupa (triple deity).",
        "en": "Cormac's Glossary of the 9th century calls Brigid the goddess poets adored, with sisters Brigid the healer and Brigid the smith; perhaps a triple deity."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "brigid-c03",
      "source_id": "brigid-s1",
      "quote": "Saint Brigid shares many of the goddess's attributes and her feast day, 1 February, was originally a festival called Imbolc. It has thus been argued that the saint is a Christianization of the goddess",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Santa Brigid (Saint Brigid) berbagi atribut sang dewi; hari rayanya 1 Februari (February) semula Imbolc, sehingga sang santa dianggap kristenisasi sang dewi.",
        "en": "Saint Brigid shares the goddess's attributes; her 1 February feast was originally Imbolc, so the saint may be a Christianization of the goddess."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "brigid-c04",
      "source_id": "brigid-s1",
      "quote": "The name comes from Proto-Celtic *Brigantī and means \"the high one\" or \"the exalted one\". It is cognate with the name of the ancient British goddess Brigantia",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nama Brigid dari Proto-Kelt (Proto-Celtic) *Brigantī, \"yang tinggi\", sekerabat dengan dewi Britania (British) Brigantia.",
        "en": "The name comes from Proto-Celtic *Brigantī, \"the high one\", cognate with the ancient British goddess Brigantia."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "brigid-c05",
      "source_id": "brigid-s1",
      "quote": "Middle Irish Brigit [ˈbʲɾʲiʝidʲ] evolved into early modern Irish Brighid [bʲɾʲiːdʲ] and, after a spelling reform in 1948, the spelling was standardised as Bríd",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Brigit (Middle Irish) menjadi Brighid, dan sejak reformasi ejaan 1948 dibakukan menjadi Bríd.",
        "en": "Middle Irish Brigit became Brighid and, after the 1948 spelling reform, Bríd."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "brigid-c06",
      "source_id": "brigid-s2",
      "quote": "Ihr Heiligtum war in Kildare (Irland), wo ihr heiliges Feuer gehütet wurde. Ihr walisischer Name lautet Ffraid",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tempat sucinya (Heiligtum) di Kildare (Irland, Irlandia), tempat api sucinya (heiliges Feuer) dijaga; nama Wales (walisischer Name) Ffraid. (Wales)",
        "en": "Her sanctuary (Heiligtum) was at Kildare (Irland) where her sacred fire (heiliges Feuer) was kept; her Welsh name (walisischer Name) is Ffraid."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "brigid-c07",
      "source_id": "brigid-s2",
      "quote": "Sie soll eine Personifikation der Dichtkunst und Beschützerin der Poeten gewesen sein und wurde in dreifacher Gestalt verehrt, weshalb manchmal von den „drei Brigids“ die Rede ist. Zusammen mit ihren beiden „Schwestern“ wird sie noch dazu als Schutzgöttin der Gesetzgeber, der Heilkunst, der Fruchtbarkeit und der Schmiede gesehen.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Brigid personifikasi puisi (Dichtkunst), pelindung penyair (Poeten), dipuja dalam tiga wujud (dreifacher Gestalt), dan pelindung pembuat hukum (Gesetzgeber), penyembuhan (Heilkunst), kesuburan, dan pandai besi (Schmiede).",
        "en": "Brigid personifies poetry (Dichtkunst), protects poets (Poeten), is worshipped in threefold form (dreifacher Gestalt), and is patron of lawgivers (Gesetzgeber), healing (Heilkunst), fertility and smiths (Schmiede)."
      }
    }

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Morrígan adalah tokoh mitologi Irlandia, dewi perang dan takdir yang meramalkan malapetaka, kematian, atau kemenangan dalam pertempuran, dan sering muncul sebagai burung gagak.",

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Morrígan atau Mórrígan, juga Morrígu, adalah tokoh dalam mitologi Irlandia (Irish mythology); namanya diterjemahkan sebagai \"ratu agung\" atau \"ratu hantu\". Ia terutama dikaitkan dengan perang dan takdir, khususnya meramalkan malapetaka, kematian, atau kemenangan dalam pertempuran, dan dalam peran itu sering muncul sebagai burung gagak, badb. Ia menghasut para pejuang untuk bertempur, membuat musuh ketakutan, dan digambarkan mencuci pakaian berlumur darah milik mereka yang ditakdirkan mati. Ia juga dipandang sebagai wujud dewi bumi dan kedaulatan, penjaga wilayah dan rakyatnya.",
      "en": "The Morrígan or Mórrígan, also Morrígu, is a figure of Irish mythology; her name is rendered \"great queen\" or \"phantom queen\". She is chiefly tied to war and fate, above all foretelling doom, death or victory in battle, and in that role often appears as a crow, the badb. She stirs warriors to fight, frightens their foes, and is shown washing the bloodied clothes of those fated to die. She is also seen as a form of the earth and sovereignty goddess, guardian of the land and its people.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "the-morrigan-c01",
        "the-morrigan-c02",
        "the-morrigan-c03"
      ]

    {
      "id": "Morrígan sering digambarkan sebagai tiga bersaudari, \"tiga Morrígna\": Badb, Macha, dan Morrígan, yang mungkin bernama Anu, dan diduga semuanya nama bagi dewi yang sama. Ia disebut istri Dagda yang pencemburu dan dewi yang dapat berubah wujud, dan dikaitkan dengan banshee dalam cerita rakyat kemudian. Menurut artikel id, ia dapat menjelma menjadi gadis cantik, pemimpin pasukan, nenek tua, burung gagak, atau burung bangkai; sebagai gagak hitam ia terbang di atas medan perang sambil memekik dan dapat meramalkan hasil pertempuran.",
      "en": "The Morrígan is often shown as three sisters, \"the three Morrígna\": Badb, Macha and the Morrígan, who may be named Anu, perhaps all names for one goddess. She is called the envious wife of the Dagda and a shape-shifting goddess, and is linked with the banshee of later folklore.       "en": "The meaning of her name is debated: mor may come from an Indo-European root meaning terror, akin to Old English maere (surviving in nightmare), and rígan means \"queen\", so \"Phantom Queen\" is now the most favoured reading.       "quote": "The Morrígan or Mórrígan, also known as Morrígu, is a figure from Irish mythology.       "id": "the-morrigan-c02",
      "source_id": "the-morrigan-s1",
      "quote": "The Morrígan encourages warriors to do brave deeds, strikes fear into their enemies, and is portrayed washing the bloodstained clothes of those fated to die.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Morrígan mendorong pejuang berani, menakuti musuh, dan mencuci pakaian berlumur darah milik yang ditakdirkan mati.",
        "en": "The Morrígan spurs warriors to brave deeds, frightens their foes and washes the bloodstained clothes of those fated to die."

    {
      "id": "the-morrigan-c03",
      "source_id": "the-morrigan-s1",
      "quote": "She is most frequently seen as a goddess of battle and war and has also been seen as a manifestation of the earth- and sovereignty-goddess, chiefly representing the goddess's role as guardian of the territory and its people.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {

      }

    {
      "id": "the-morrigan-c04",
      "source_id": "the-morrigan-s1",
      "quote": "The Morrígan is often described as a trio of individuals, all sisters, called \"the three Morrígna\". In mythology membership of the triad is given as Badb, Macha, and the Morrigan, who may be named Anu. It is believed that these were all names for the same goddess.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Morrígan sering digambarkan sebagai tiga bersaudari (three Morrígna): Badb, Macha, dan Morrigan yang mungkin bernama Anu, mungkin nama dewi yang sama.",
        "en": "The Morrígan is often three sisters, the three Morrígna: Badb, Macha and the Morrigan, who may be named Anu, perhaps names of one goddess."

      "source_id": "the-morrigan-s1",

      "source_id": "the-morrigan-s2",
      "quote": "Ia dapat mengubah wujudnya menjadi seorang gadis rupawan, pemimpin pasukan, nenek tua, dan burung gagak maupun burung bangkai (hering).",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Morrigan dapat menjelma gadis rupawan, pemimpin pasukan, nenek tua, burung gagak, atau burung bangkai (hering).",
        "en": "The Morrigan can become a fair maiden, war leader, old woman, crow or vulture (burung bangkai)."

    {

      "source_id": "the-morrigan-s2",
      "quote": "Citra gagak hitam sering muncul dalam suatu peperangan yang mana ia terbang diatas medan perang sambil mengeluarkan lengkingannya dan mampu meramalkan hasil peperangan tersebut.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebagai gagak hitam ia terbang di atas medan perang sambil melengking dan meramalkan hasil peperangan.",
        "en": "As a black crow (gagak hitam) she flies over the battlefield (medan perang) shrieking and foretells the outcome (hasil peperangan)."
      }

    {
      "id": "the-morrigan-c09",
      "source_id": "the-morrigan-s1",
      "quote": "Mor may derive from an Indo-European root connoting terror, monstrousness, cognate with the Old English maere (which survives in the modern English word \"nightmare\") and the Scandinavian mara",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Unsur Mor mungkin dari akar Indo-Eropa (Indo-European) bermakna teror, sekerabat dengan maere (Old English, Inggris Kuno) dalam kata nightmare dan mara Skandinavia (Scandinavian).",
        "en": "Mor may come from an Indo-European root of terror, akin to Old English maere (in nightmare) and Scandinavian mara."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "the-morrigan-c10",
      "source_id": "the-morrigan-s1",
      "quote": "The earliest sources for the Morrígan are glosses in Latin manuscripts and glossaries (collections of glosses). The 8th century O'Mulconry's Glossary says that Macha is one of the three morrígna.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {

        "en": "The earliest sources are glosses in Latin manuscripts; the 8th-century O'Mulconry's Glossary names Macha one of the three morrígna."

    },
    {
      "id": "the-morrigan-c11",
      "source_id": "the-morrigan-s1",
      "quote": "while rígan translates as \"queen\". This etymological sequence can be reconstructed in the Proto-Celtic language as *Moro-rīganī-s. Accordingly, Morrígan is often translated as \"Phantom Queen\". This is the derivation generally favoured in current scholarship.",
      "locator": null,

    "id": "Danu adalah dewi yang diduga ada dalam mitologi Irlandia, yang satu-satunya jejaknya adalah nama Tuatha Dé Danann, \"bangsa dewi Danu\".",

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Danu diduga merupakan dewi dalam mitologi Irlandia (Irish mythology), yang satu-satunya kesaksian adalah nama Tuatha Dé Danann, biasa diterjemahkan \"bangsa dewi Danu\". Meski tokoh itu sama sekali tidak muncul dalam teks primer, sebagian folkloris era Victoria memberinya sifat-sifat seperti keibuan atau kemakmuran pertanian. Bentuk nominatif *Danu adalah rekonstruksi para sarjana modern dari bentuk genitif Danann, satu-satunya bentuk yang tercatat, dan tidak ada mitos atau legenda abad pertengahan tentang dirinya.",
      "en": "Danu is presumed to have been a goddess of Irish mythology, attested only in the name of the Tuatha Dé Danann, usually rendered \"the folk of the goddess Danu\". Although no such figure appears in the primary texts, some later Victorian folklorists gave her traits such as motherhood or farming prosperity. The nominative *Danu is a modern scholarly reconstruction from the genitive Danann, the only attested form, and no medieval myths or legends about her survive.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "danu-c01",
        "danu-c02",
        "danu-c05"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Dalam mitologi Irlandia, Anu adalah seorang dewi; ia mungkin dewi tersendiri atau nama lain Danu. Cormac's Glossary menyebut Anu sebagai ibu para dewa, dan sebagian sarjana mengira Danu merupakan perpaduan dengan Anu. Asal namanya diperdebatkan: sarjana awal mengaitkannya dengan dewi air Weda Danu dan akar kata \"mengalir\", yang mungkin juga menjadi asal nama sungai Danube. Ia mungkin sepadan dengan tokoh Wales Dôn dalam Mabinogion. Menurut artikel de, nama Danu sendiri tidak pernah muncul dalam mitologi Irlandia Kuno, melainkan disimpulkan dari istilah Túatha Dé Danann dan Tri Dée Dána.",
      "en": "In Irish mythology Anu is a goddess; she may be distinct or another name for Danu. Cormac's Glossary names Anu as mother of the gods, and some scholars think Danu is a blending with Anu. The origin of the name is debated: early scholars linked it to the Vedic water goddess Danu and a root meaning \"to flow\", which may also underlie the river name Danube. She may parallel the Welsh figure Dôn of the Mabinogion. According to the de article, the name Danu itself never appears in Old Irish mythology but is inferred from the terms Túatha Dé Danann and Tri Dée Dána.",

      "quote": "is presumed to have been a goddess in Irish mythology, whose sole attestation is in the name of the Tuatha Dé Danann, which is usually translated 'the folk of the goddess Danu'. Despite the complete absence of any such figure from the primary texts, some later Victorian folklorists attempted to ascribe certain attributes to Danu, such as association with motherhood or agricultural prosperity.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Danu diduga dewi mitologi Irlandia (Irish mythology), hanya tercatat dalam nama Tuatha Dé Danann; folkloris era Victoria (Victorian) memberinya sifat keibuan dan kemakmuran.",
        "en": "Danu is presumed a goddess of Irish mythology, attested only in the name Tuatha Dé Danann; Victorian folklorists gave her traits of motherhood and prosperity."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "danu-c02",
      "source_id": "danu-s1",
      "quote": "The hypothetical nominative form of the name, *Danu, is not found in any medieval Irish text, but is rather a reconstruction by modern scholars based on the genitive Danann (also spelled Donand or Danand), which is the only form attested in the primary sources",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Bentuk *Danu tak ditemukan dalam teks Irlandia (Irish) abad pertengahan, melainkan rekonstruksi dari genitif Danann.",
        "en": "The form *Danu is not in any medieval Irish text but is reconstructed from the genitive Danann."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "danu-c03",
      "source_id": "danu-s1",
      "quote": "In Irish mythology, Anu (sometimes declined as Anann or Anand) is a goddess. She may be a distinct goddess in her own right or an alternative name for Danu",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam mitologi Irlandia (Irish), Anu adalah dewi tersendiri atau nama lain Danu.",
        "en": "In Irish mythology Anu is a goddess, distinct or another name for Danu."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "danu-c04",
      "source_id": "danu-s1",
      "quote": "Cormac's Glossary, a text that predates the Lebor Gabala Erenn, names the goddess Anu as the mother of the gods. Some scholars suggest that Danu was a conflation of Anu and is the same goddess.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Cormac's Glossary, lebih tua dari Lebor Gabala Erenn, menyebut Anu ibu para dewa; sebagian sarjana menyamakan Danu dengan Anu.",
        "en": "Cormac's Glossary, older than the Lebor Gabala Erenn, names Anu mother of the gods; some scholars merge Danu with Anu."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "danu-c05",
      "source_id": "danu-s1",
      "quote": "Danu has no surviving myths or legends associated with her in any of the medieval Irish texts.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tidak ada mitos atau legenda tentang Danu dalam teks Irlandia (Irish) abad pertengahan.",
        "en": "No myths or legends about Danu survive in medieval Irish texts."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "danu-c06",
      "source_id": "danu-s1",
      "quote": "with some earlier scholars favoring a link with the Vedic water goddess Danu, whose name is derived from the Proto-Indo-European root *dʰenh₂- \"to run, to flow\", which may also lie behind the ancient name for the river Danube",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sarjana awal mengaitkannya dengan dewi air Weda (Vedic) Danu dari akar Proto-Indo-Eropa (Proto-Indo-European) \"mengalir\", mungkin juga asal nama sungai Danube.",
        "en": "Early scholars linked it to the Vedic water goddess Danu from a Proto-Indo-European root to flow, perhaps behind the river name Danube."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "danu-c07",
      "source_id": "danu-s2",
      "quote": "Der Name Danu selbst taucht in der altirischen Mythologie als solcher niemals auf, wird aber aus den tradierten Begriffen Túatha Dé Danann („Die Stämme der Göttin Danu“) und Tri Dée Dána („Die drei Götter von Dána“) abgeleitet.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nama (Name) Danu tak pernah muncul dalam mitologi Irlandia Kuno (altirischen Mythologie), tetapi disimpulkan dari Túatha Dé Danann dan Tri Dée Dána.",
        "en": "The name (Name) Danu never appears in Old Irish mythology (altirischen Mythologie) but is inferred from Túatha Dé Danann and Tri Dée Dána."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "danu-c08",
      "source_id": "danu-s1",
      "quote": "She has possible parallels with the Welsh legendary figure Dôn in the medieval tales of the Mabinogion, whom most modern scholars consider to be a mythological mother goddess.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Danu mungkin sepadan dengan tokoh legenda Wales (Welsh) Dôn dalam Mabinogion, yang dianggap dewi ibu.",
        "en": "Danu may parallel the Welsh legendary figure Dôn of the Mabinogion, seen as a mother goddess."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "danu-c09",
      "source_id": "danu-s1",
      "quote": "This may also connect Danu to The Morrígan, which some scholars say is an epithet for Anu.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Hal ini mungkin juga menghubungkan Danu dengan The Morrígan, yang oleh sebagian sarjana dianggap julukan Anu.",
        "en": "This may also link Danu to The Morrígan, which some scholars call an epithet of Anu."
      }
    }
  ],

    "id": "Dagda dianggap dewa agung mitologi Irlandia, pemimpin Tuatha Dé Danann yang digambarkan sebagai bapak, raja, dan druid.",
    "en": "The Dagda is considered the great god of Irish mythology, chief of the Tuatha Dé Danann, portrayed as father-figure, king and druid.",

    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dagda (Old Irish: In Dagda; bahasa Irlandia: An Daghdha) dianggap dewa agung mitologi Irlandia (Irish mythology) dan dewa utama Tuatha Dé Danann, digambarkan sebagai sosok bapak, raja, dan druid. Ia dikaitkan dengan kesuburan, pertanian, kejantanan dan kekuatan, serta sihir, kedruidan, dan kebijaksanaan; ia dapat menguasai hidup dan mati, cuaca dan panen, serta waktu dan musim. Ia sering digambarkan sebagai lelaki besar berjanggut atau raksasa berjubah berkerudung.",
      "en": "The Dagda (Old Irish: In Dagda; Irish: An Daghdha) is regarded as the great god of Irish mythology and chief god of the Tuatha Dé Danann, a father-figure, king and druid. He is tied to fertility, agriculture, manliness and strength, and to magic, druidry and wisdom; he commands life and death, weather and crops, time and the seasons. He is often pictured as a big bearded man or giant in a hooded cloak.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "the-dagda-c01",
        "the-dagda-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ia memiliki tongkat sakti (lorc) berwatak ganda yang membunuh dengan satu ujung dan menghidupkan dengan ujung lain, kuali (coire ansic) yang tak pernah kosong, dan harpa sakti yang dapat terbang kepadanya bila dipanggil dengan dua julukannya. Ia dikatakan tinggal di Brú na Bóinne (Newgrange). Ia suami Morrígan dan kekasih Boann, dan anak-anaknya antara lain Aengus, Brigit, Bodb Derg, Cermait, Aed, dan Midir. Namanya diduga berarti \"dewa yang baik\" atau \"dewa agung\", dari kata Proto-Kelt *Dago-dēwos, dan ia dibandingkan dengan Odin, Sucellos dari Galia, serta Dīs Pater atau Jupiter.",
      "en": "He owns a magic staff (lorc) of double nature that kills with one end and revives with the other, a cauldron (coire ansic) that never runs empty, and a magic harp that flies to him when called by its two bynames. He is said to dwell at Brú na Bóinne (Newgrange). He is the husband of the Morrígan and lover of Boann, and his children include Aengus, Brigit, Bodb Derg, Cermait, Aed and Midir. His name, from Proto-Celtic *Dago-dēwos, is usually explained as \"good god\" or \"great god\", and he has been likened to Odin, the Gaulish Sucellos, and Dīs Pater or Jupiter.",

      "id": "the-dagda-c05",
      "source_id": "the-dagda-s1",
      "quote": "The Dagda's name is thought to mean \"the good god\" or \"the great god\". His other names include Eochu or Eochaid Ollathair (\"horseman, great father\"), and Ruad Rofhessa (\"mighty one/lord of great knowledge\").",

    {
      "id": "the-dagda-c06",
      "source_id": "the-dagda-s1",
      "quote": "The Dagda is said to be the husband of the Morrígan and lover of Boann. His children include Aengus, Brigit, Bodb Derg, Cermait, Aed, and Midir.",

      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {

    {
      "id": "the-dagda-c07",
      "source_id": "the-dagda-s1",
      "quote": "The Dagda has been likened to the Germanic god Odin, the Gaulish god Sucellos, and the Roman god Dīs Pater or Jupiter.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dagda dibandingkan dengan dewa Jermanik (Germanic) Odin, dewa Galia (Gaulish) Sucellos, dan Dīs Pater atau Jupiter (Roman).",
        "en": "The Dagda is likened to the Germanic Odin, the Gaulish Sucellos and the Roman Dīs Pater or Jupiter."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "the-dagda-c08",
      "source_id": "the-dagda-s2",
      "quote": "Dagda ist ein Anführer der mythischen Túatha Dé Danann. Er ist der Allvater und zuständig für Recht, Gesetz und Ordnung. Er wird als hässlich mit einem Schmerbauch beschrieben. Er trägt eine Kapuze, mit der er sein Gesicht verhüllt, und eine Tunika.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dagda pemimpin (Anführer) Túatha Dé Danann, Allvater yang mengurus hukum (Gesetz) dan ketertiban (Ordnung), buruk rupa (hässlich) berperut buncit (Schmerbauch), berkerudung (Kapuze) dan bertunik (Tunika).",
        "en": "The Dagda is a leader (Anführer) of the Túatha Dé Danann, the Allvater over law (Gesetz) and order (Ordnung), ugly (hässlich) and pot-bellied (Schmerbauch), with hood (Kapuze) and tunic (Tunika)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "the-dagda-c09",
      "source_id": "the-dagda-s2",
      "quote": "Seine Attribute sind der „magische Kessel“, die „magische Harfe“ und die „magische Keule“.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Atributnya (Attribute) kuali sakti (magische Kessel), harpa sakti (magische Harfe), dan gada sakti (magische Keule).",

      }
    },
    {
      "id": "the-dagda-c10",
      "source_id": "the-dagda-s1",
      "quote": "The Old Irish name Dagda is generally believed to stem from Proto-Celtic: *Dago-dēwos , meaning \"the good god\" or \"the great god\".",

      "statement": {

        "en": "The Old Irish name Dagda stems from Proto-Celtic *Dago-dēwos."
      }

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Fomoria adalah bangsa gaib dalam mitologi Irlandia yang sering digambarkan bermusuhan dan mengerikan, berasal dari bawah laut atau bumi, dan dikalahkan Tuatha Dé Danann di Mag Tuired.",
    "en": "The Fomorians are a supernatural race of Irish mythology, often shown as hostile and monstrous, said to come from under the sea or earth, and defeated by the Tuatha Dé Danann at Mag Tuired.",

      "fomorians-c01",

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Fomoria atau Fomori (Old Irish: Fomóire; Modern Irish: Fomhóraigh) adalah bangsa gaib dalam mitologi Irlandia (Irish mythology) yang sering digambarkan bermusuhan dan mengerikan. Awalnya mereka dikatakan datang dari bawah laut atau bumi; kemudian mereka digambarkan sebagai perompak laut dan raksasa. Mereka musuh para pemukim pertama Irlandia dan lawan Tuatha Dé Danann, walau sebagian anggota kedua bangsa beranak bersama. Tuatha Dé Danann mengalahkan Fomoria dalam pertempuran Mag Tuired, yang dibandingkan dengan mitos perang para dewa lainnya.",
      "en": "The Fomorians or Fomori (Old Irish: Fomóire; Modern Irish: Fomhóraigh) are a supernatural race of Irish mythology, often shown as hostile and monstrous. Early tradition places their origin beneath the waves or below ground; afterwards they became sea-raiders and giants. They fought the earliest settlers of Ireland and opposed the Tuatha Dé Danann, even though some members of both races had children together. The Tuatha Dé Danann defeat them at the Battle of Mag Tuired, a clash likened to other myths of wars among the gods.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "fomorians-c01",
        "fomorians-c02",
        "fomorians-c03",
        "fomorians-c09",
        "fomorians-c10"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Satu teori menganggap Fomoria makhluk gaib yang mewakili kekuatan alam yang liar atau merusak: personifikasi kekacauan, kegelapan, kematian, hama, dan kekeringan. Unsur pertama nama mereka umumnya dianggap fo (\"di bawah\"), sedangkan unsur kedua mungkin berarti laut, besar, atau hantu. Dalam salah satu rujukan tertua, ratapan abad ke-7 untuk Mess-Telmann, mereka disebut tinggal \"di bawah dunia manusia\". Mereka kadang digambarkan bertubuh manusia berkepala kambing, atau bermata, berlengan, dan berkaki satu. Menurut artikel de, padanannya di Skotlandia disebut Foawr, dan mereka tampil sebagai raksasa iblis berlengan dan berkaki satu, sebagian berkepala hewan.",
      "en": "One theory holds that the Fomorians stood for the wild or destructive powers of nature: chaos, darkness, death, blight and drought. The first part of their name is usually taken as fo (\"under\"), while the second may mean sea, great or phantom. In one of the earliest references, a likely 7th-century elegy for Mess-Telmann, they dwell \"under the worlds of men\". They are sometimes given a man's body with a goat's head, or one eye, one arm and one leg. According to the de article they are known in Scotland as Foawr, and appear as demonic giants with one arm and one leg, some with animal heads.",

    {
      "id": "fomorians-c03",
      "source_id": "fomorians-s1",
      "quote": "They are enemies of Ireland's first settlers and opponents of the Tuatha Dé Danann, the other supernatural race in Irish mythology; although some members of the two races have offspring. The Tuatha Dé Danann defeat the Fomorians in the Battle of Mag Tuired.",

      "source_id": "fomorians-s1",

      "source_id": "fomorians-s1",

    {
      "id": "fomorians-c07",
      "source_id": "fomorians-s1",
      "quote": "Sometimes they are said to have the body of a man and the head of a goat, according to an 11th-century text in Lebor na hUidre (the Book of the Dun Cow), or to have had one eye, one arm and one leg.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {

      "id": "fomorians-c08",
      "source_id": "fomorians-s2",
      "quote": "In Schottland sind sie als „Foawr“ bekannt. Sie erscheinen als dämonische Riesen mit nur einem Arm und einem Bein und einige Angehörige ihres Volkes haben Tierköpfe (Ziegen-, Hirsch- oder Stier-), sind gehörnt oder reptilienartig.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di Skotlandia (Schottland) mereka disebut Foawr; raksasa iblis (dämonische Riesen) berlengan (Arm) dan berkaki (Bein) satu, sebagian berkepala hewan (Tierköpfe).",
        "en": "In Scotland (Schottland) they are called Foawr; demonic giants (dämonische Riesen) with one arm (Arm) and leg (Bein), some with animal heads (Tierköpfe)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "fomorians-c09",
      "source_id": "fomorians-s2",
      "quote": "Die Tuatha de Danann entrissen den Firbolg bei der ersten Schlacht von Magh Tuireadh die Herrschaft über Irland und mussten dann bei der zweiten Schlacht von Magh Tuireadh die Fomorii schlagen, um die Eroberung zu sichern.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tuatha de Danann merebut Irlandia (Irland) dari Firbolg pada pertempuran pertama Magh Tuireadh dan mengalahkan Fomorii pada pertempuran kedua (zweiten Schlacht).",
        "en": "The Tuatha de Danann took Ireland (Irland) from the Firbolg at the first battle of Magh Tuireadh and beat the Fomorii at the second (zweiten Schlacht)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "fomorians-c10",
      "source_id": "fomorians-s1",
      "quote": "This has been likened to other Indo-European myths of a war between gods, such as the Æsir and Vanir War in Norse mythology, the Titanomachy (Olympian-Titan War) in Greek mythology, and the Devas and Asuras in Indian mythology.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pertempuran ini dibandingkan dengan mitos perang para dewa lainnya, seperti perang Æsir dan Vanir dan Titanomachy.",
        "en": "This is likened to other myths of a war between gods, such as the Æsir–Vanir War and the Titanomachy."
      }

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Aos sí adalah nama Irlandia untuk ras gaib dalam cerita rakyat Gaelik, mirip elf, yang konon keturunan Tuatha Dé Danann dan tinggal di gundukan peri (sí).",
    "en": "Aos sí is the Irish name for a supernatural race of Gaelic folklore, similar to elves, said to descend from the Tuatha Dé Danann and to live in fairy mounds (sí).",
    "claim_ids": [
      "aos-si-c01",
      "aos-si-c02"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Aos sí atau aos sídhe adalah nama Irlandia untuk ras gaib dalam cerita rakyat Gaelik (Gaelic folklore), mirip elf. Mereka dikatakan keturunan Tuatha Dé Danann atau dewa-dewa mitologi Irlandia. Istilah aos sí berarti \"bangsa sí\", yaitu gundukan tempat mereka tinggal, yang dipandang sebagai gerbang ke Dunia Lain; dalam bahasa Inggris tempat itu disebut fairy mounds atau hollow hills. Mereka berinteraksi dengan manusia dan disebut sebagai leluhur, roh alam, atau dewi dan dewa.",
      "en": "In Irish, aos sí or aos sídhe names an elf-like otherworldly people of Gaelic lore, held to be the offspring of the Tuatha Dé Danann or of the old Irish gods. The term means \"folk of the sí\", the mounds they dwell in, seen as gateways to an Otherworld; in English these are called fairy mounds or hollow hills. They deal with humans and are variously called ancestors, nature spirits, or goddesses and gods.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "aos-si-c01",
        "aos-si-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Nama lainnya daoine sí, juga \"orang gundukan peri\"; dalam bahasa Inggris mereka sering disebut \"the Sidhe\". Pada abad ke-7 uskup Tírechán menyebut kaum sídh sebagai \"dewa-dewa bumi\", dan Fiacc's Hymn abad ke-8 menyebut orang Irlandia memuja mereka sebelum kedatangan Santo Patrick. Dalam cerita rakyat Irlandia, peri bisa berasal dari malaikat yang jatuh tetapi dosanya tak cukup berat untuk neraka, atau dari Tuatha Dé Danann yang mundur ke Dunia Lain setelah kalah dari putra-putra Míl Espáine. Mereka sering ditenangkan dengan persembahan, dan orang berhati-hati agar tak membuat mereka marah.",
      "en": "Another name is daoine sí, likewise \"people of the fairy mound\"; in English they are often simply \"the Sidhe\". In the 7th century bishop Tírechán called the sídh folk \"earthly gods\", and the 8th-century Fiacc's Hymn says the Irish worshipped them before Saint Patrick came. In Irish folklore fairies may be fallen angels whose sins were too slight for hell, or descendants of the Tuatha Dé Danann who withdrew into the Otherworld after losing to the Sons of Míl Espáine. They are often appeased with offerings, and people take care not to anger them.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "aos-si-c03",
        "aos-si-c04",
        "aos-si-c05",
        "aos-si-c06",
        "aos-si-c09"
      ]

      "id": "Menurut artikel id, aos sí adalah ras supranatural dalam mitologi Irlandia dan Skotlandia yang tinggal di bawah tanah atau di dunia tak kasatmata yang berdampingan dengan dunia manusia; Lebor Gabála Érenn menggambarkan dunia itu sebagai alam semesta paralel. Dalam mitologi Skotlandia mereka disebut daoine sìth.",
      "en": "According to the id article, the aos sí are a supernatural race of Irish and Scottish mythology living underground or in an invisible world alongside ours, which the Lebor Gabála Érenn describes as a parallel universe. In Scottish mythology they are called daoine sìth.",

    "id": "Pada abad ke-7 uskup Tírechán menyebut kaum sídh sebagai \"dewa-dewa bumi\" (dei terreni).",
    "en": "In the 7th century bishop Tírechán called the sídh folk \"earthly gods\" (dei terreni).",

  "modern_depictions": [],

    {
      "id": "aos-si-s1",

      "accessed": "2026-10-02"
    },
    {
      "id": "aos-si-s2",
      "url": "https://id.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aos_S%C3%AD",
      "title": "Aos Sí",
      "author": null,
      "publisher": "Wikipedia (id)",
      "published": null,
      "language": "id",
      "type": "wikipedia",
      "accessed": "2026-10-02"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "aos-si-c01",
      "source_id": "aos-si-s1",
      "quote": "is the Irish name for a supernatural race in Gaelic folklore, similar to elves. They are said to descend from the Tuatha Dé Danann or the gods of Irish mythology.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Aos sí adalah nama Irlandia (Irish) untuk ras gaib dalam cerita rakyat Gaelik (Gaelic folklore), mirip elf, keturunan Tuatha Dé Danann atau dewa mitologi Irlandia.",
        "en": "Aos sí is the Irish name for a supernatural race of Gaelic folklore, similar to elves, descended from the Tuatha Dé Danann or Irish gods."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "aos-si-c02",
      "source_id": "aos-si-s1",
      "quote": "The term aos sí means \"folk of the sí\"; these are the mounds in which they are said to dwell, which are seen as portals to an Otherworld. Such abodes are referred to in English as \"shees\", \"fairy mounds\", \"elf mounds\" or \"hollow hills\". The aos sí interact with humans and the human world. They are variously said to be the ancestors, the spirits of nature, or goddesses and gods.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Aos sí berarti bangsa sí, gundukan gerbang ke Dunia Lain (Otherworld), dalam bahasa Inggris (English) fairy mounds atau hollow hills; mereka disebut leluhur, roh alam, atau dewa-dewi.",
        "en": "Aos sí means folk of the sí, mounds that are portals to an Otherworld, in English fairy mounds or hollow hills; they are called ancestors, nature spirits, or gods."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "aos-si-c03",
      "source_id": "aos-si-s1",
      "quote": "Another name for the aos sí is daoine sí , which likewise means \"people of the fairy mound\".",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nama lain aos sí adalah daoine sí, orang gundukan peri; dalam bahasa Inggris \"the Sidhe\".",
        "en": "Another name is daoine sí, people of the fairy mound; in English \"the Sidhe\"."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "aos-si-c04",
      "source_id": "aos-si-s1",
      "quote": "Writing in the 7th century, the Irish bishop Tírechán described the sídh folk as \"earthly gods\" (Latin: dei terreni ). The 8th century Fiacc's Hymn says that the Irish adored the sídh folk before the coming of Saint Patrick.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Uskup Irlandia (Irish) Tírechán abad ke-7 menyebut kaum sídh dewa bumi (dei terreni, Latin); Fiacc's Hymn abad ke-8 menyebut mereka dipuja sebelum Saint Patrick (Santo Patrick).",
        "en": "The 7th-century Irish bishop Tírechán called the sídh folk earthly gods (dei terreni); the 8th-century Fiacc's Hymn says they were adored before Saint Patrick."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "aos-si-c05",
      "source_id": "aos-si-s1",
      "quote": "The fairies could either be fallen angels or the descendants of the Tuatha Dé Danann; in the latter case, this is equivalent with aos sí.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Peri bisa malaikat yang jatuh atau keturunan Tuatha Dé Danann, yang sama dengan aos sí.",
        "en": "Fairies could be fallen angels or descendants of the Tuatha Dé Danann, equivalent to the aos sí."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "aos-si-c06",
      "source_id": "aos-si-s1",
      "quote": "In folk belief and practice, the aos sí are often appeased with offerings and care is taken to avoid angering or insulting them.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam kepercayaan rakyat, aos sí ditenangkan dengan persembahan dan orang menghindari membuat mereka marah.",
        "en": "In folk belief the aos sí are appeased with offerings and care is taken not to anger them."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "aos-si-c07",
      "source_id": "aos-si-s2",
      "quote": "adalah istilah yang mengacu kepada ras supranatural dalam mitologi Irlandia dan Skotlandia yang mirip dengan peri atau elf. Konon mereka tinggal di bawah tanah atau di dunia kasatmata yang ada bersamaan dengan dunia manusia. Dunia ini dijelaskan di dalam Lebor Gabála Érenn sebagai alam semesta paralel",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Aos sí ras supranatural mitologi Irlandia dan Skotlandia mirip peri atau elf, tinggal di bawah tanah atau dunia lain di samping dunia manusia, yang dalam Lebor Gabála Érenn adalah alam semesta paralel.",
        "en": "The aos sí are a supernatural race of Irish and Scottish (Irlandia, Skotlandia) mythology like fairies or elves, living underground or in a world beside ours, a parallel universe in the Lebor Gabála Érenn."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "aos-si-c08",
      "source_id": "aos-si-s2",
      "quote": "sementara dalam mitologi Skotlandia mereka dinamai daoine sìth.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam mitologi Skotlandia mereka dinamai daoine sìth.",
        "en": "In Scottish (Skotlandia) mythology they are called daoine sìth."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "aos-si-c09",
      "source_id": "aos-si-s1",
      "quote": "Some sources describe them as the survivors of the Tuatha Dé Danann who retreated into the Otherworld when fleeing the mortal Sons of Míl Espáine",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebagian sumber menyebut mereka sisa Tuatha Dé Danann yang mundur ke Dunia Lain (Otherworld) saat lari dari putra-putra Míl Espáine (kaum Milesia).",
        "en": "Some sources call them survivors of the Tuatha Dé Danann who retreated into the Otherworld from the Sons of Míl Espáine."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Familiar adalah makhluk gaib dalam cerita rakyat Eropa abad pertengahan dan awal modern yang dipercaya melindungi atau membantu penyihir dan dukun dalam sihir dan ramalan, biasanya berwujud hewan.",
    "en": "A familiar is a supernatural entity of medieval and early modern European folklore believed to protect or help witches and cunning folk in magic and divination, usually in animal form.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "familiar-c01",
      "familiar-c02"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam cerita rakyat Eropa (European folklore) abad pertengahan dan awal modern, familiar (tepatnya familiar spirits) dipercaya sebagai makhluk gaib atau penjaga roh yang melindungi atau membantu penyihir dan cunning folk dalam sihir, ramalan, dan wawasan spiritual. Catatan masa itu menyebut familiar dapat muncul dalam banyak wujud, biasanya hewan, kadang manusia, dan digambarkan jelas, tiga dimensi, berwarna, dan bergerak, berbeda dari hantu yang berwujud asap. Familiar penyihir sering dianggap jahat dan digolongkan iblis, sedangkan familiar cunning folk dianggap baik dan lebih sering disebut peri.",
      "en": "In medieval and early modern European folklore, familiars (strictly familiar spirits) were believed to be supernatural beings or spirit guardians that protected or helped witches and cunning folk in magic, divination and spiritual insight. Records of the time say they could take many forms, usually an animal, sometimes a human, and were vivid, three-dimensional, coloured and moving, unlike smoky ghosts. A witch's familiar was often thought malevolent and classed as a demon, while a cunning man's was thought kindly and more often called a fairy.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "familiar-c01",
        "familiar-c02",
        "familiar-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Tujuan utama familiar adalah melayani penyihir dan melindunginya saat ia mulai memperoleh kekuatannya. Sejak abad ke-20, sebagian praktisi sihir, termasuk penganut Wicca, memakai konsep familiar, memanfaatkan hewan peliharaan atau satwa liar, atau meyakini familiar tak kasatmata sebagai pembantu magis. Menurut artikel de, familiar atau Hexentier adalah makhluk kecil berwujud hewan terutama dalam cerita rakyat Kelt dan Anglo-Saxon; mereka tidak terikat pada rumah dan dapat bepergian bersama pemiliknya, dan sebagian pemburu penyihir melihatnya sebagai wujud iblis atau Setan.",
      "en": "The main purpose of a familiar was to serve the witch and protect her as she came into her powers. Since the 20th century some magic practitioners, including Wiccans, use the idea of familiars, working with pets or wildlife or believing in invisible familiars as magical helpers. According to the de article, the familiar or Hexentier is a small animal-shaped being mainly of Celtic and Anglo-Saxon folklore; it is not bound to a house and can travel with its owner, and some witch-hunters saw it as an earthly form of a demon or the Devil.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "familiar-c04",
        "familiar-c05",
        "familiar-c06",
        "familiar-c07",
        "familiar-c08"
      ]

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "familiar-c01",
      "source_id": "familiar-s1",
      "quote": "were believed to be supernatural entities, interdimensional beings, or spiritual guardians that would protect or assist witches and cunning folk in their practice of magic, divination, and spiritual insight.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam cerita rakyat Eropa (European folklore), familiar (familiar spirits) adalah makhluk gaib yang melindungi atau membantu penyihir dan cunning folk dalam sihir dan ramalan.",
        "en": "In European folklore, familiars (familiar spirits) were supernatural beings protecting or helping witches and cunning folk in magic and divination."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "familiar-c02",
      "source_id": "familiar-s1",
      "quote": "those alleging to have had contact with familiar spirits reported that they could manifest as numerous forms, usually as an animal, but sometimes as a human or humanoid figure",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Familiar dapat muncul dalam banyak wujud, biasanya hewan, kadang manusia.",
        "en": "Familiars could appear in many forms, usually an animal, sometimes a human."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "familiar-c03",
      "source_id": "familiar-s1",
      "quote": "When they served witches, they were often thought to be malevolent, but when working for cunning folk, they were often considered benevolent (although there was some ambiguity in both cases). The former were often categorized as demons, while the latter were more commonly thought of and described as fairies.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Familiar penyihir dianggap jahat dan digolongkan iblis; familiar cunning folk dianggap baik dan disebut peri.",
        "en": "Witches' familiars were thought malevolent and classed as demons; cunning folk's were benevolent and called fairies."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "familiar-c04",
      "source_id": "familiar-s1",
      "quote": "The main purpose of familiars was to serve the witch, providing protection for them as they came into their new powers.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tujuan utama familiar melayani dan melindungi penyihir.",
        "en": "The main purpose of familiars was to serve and protect the witch."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "familiar-c05",
      "source_id": "familiar-s1",
      "quote": "Since the 20th century some magical practitioners, including adherents of the neopagan religion of Wicca, use the concept of familiars, due to their association with older forms of magic. These contemporary practitioners use pets or wildlife, or believe that invisible versions of familiars act as magical aides.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sejak abad ke-20 praktisi sihir, termasuk penganut Wicca, memakai konsep familiar dengan hewan peliharaan atau familiar tak kasatmata.",
        "en": "Since the 20th century practitioners, including Wiccans, use familiars, with pets or invisible familiars as aides."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "familiar-c06",
      "source_id": "familiar-s2",
      "quote": "Als Familiar (englisch für Vertraute/r) oder Hexentier – mitunter auch lateinisch spiritus familiaris – werden besonders in der keltischen und angelsächsischen Folklore kleine übernatürliche Wesen mit tierischer Gestalt bezeichnet, die oftmals Hexen und Heilerinnen zur Seite stehen und diese unterstützen sollen.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Familiar atau Hexentier (spiritus familiaris) adalah makhluk gaib kecil berwujud hewan (tierischer Gestalt) dalam cerita rakyat Kelt (keltischen) dan Anglo-Saxon (angelsächsischen) yang membantu penyihir (Hexen) dan penyembuh (Heilerinnen).",
        "en": "The Familiar or Hexentier (spiritus familiaris) is a small animal-shaped (tierischer Gestalt) supernatural being of Celtic (keltischen) and Anglo-Saxon (angelsächsischen) folklore aiding witches (Hexen) and healers (Heilerinnen)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "familiar-c07",
      "source_id": "familiar-s2",
      "quote": "Sie sind nicht an ein Haus und Grundstück gebunden und können mit ihrer Besitzerin reisen.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Familiar tidak terikat pada rumah (Haus) dan dapat bepergian (reisen) bersama pemiliknya (Besitzerin).",
        "en": "Familiars are not bound to a house (Haus) and can travel (reisen) with their owner (Besitzerin)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "familiar-c08",
      "source_id": "familiar-s2",
      "quote": "Manche Hexenjäger sahen in den Familiars nicht nur einen Beweis für die Wahrheit ihrer Theorie, dass eine beschuldigte Person eine Hexe war, sondern in den Familiars eine irdische Gestalt eines Dämons oder des Teufels selbst.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebagian pemburu penyihir (Hexenjäger) melihat Familiars sebagai bukti dan wujud iblis (Dämons) atau Setan (Teufels).",
        "en": "Some witch-hunters (Hexenjäger) saw Familiars as proof and as an earthly form of a demon (Dämons) or the Devil (Teufels)."
      }
    }

    "id": "Fionn mac Cumhaill (Finn McCool) adalah pahlawan mitologi Irlandia dan cerita rakyat Skotlandia serta Man, pemimpin kelompok pemburu-pejuang Fianna, juga peramal dan penyair.",
    "en": "Fionn mac Cumhaill (Finn McCool) is a hero of Irish mythology and later Scottish and Manx folklore, leader of the Fianna hunter-warrior bands, and also a seer and poet.",

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Fionn mac Cumhaill (juga Finn mac Cumhaill, Finn McCool, atau MacCool) adalah pahlawan dalam mitologi Irlandia (Irish mythology) serta cerita rakyat Skotlandia dan Man kemudian. Ia pemimpin kelompok pemburu-pejuang muda Fianna, sekaligus peramal dan penyair, dan konon memiliki ibu jari ajaib yang memberinya kebijaksanaan besar. Ia sering digambarkan berburu bersama anjingnya Bran dan Sceólang serta bertarung dengan tombak dan pedang. Kisah-kisahnya membentuk Siklus Fianna (Fenian Cycle), sebagian besar dituturkan oleh putranya, penyair Oisín.",
      "en": "Fionn mac Cumhaill (also Finn mac Cumhaill, Finn McCool or MacCool) is a hero of Irish mythology and of later Scottish and Manx folklore. He leads the Fianna, bands of young roving hunter-warriors, and is also a seer and poet, said to have a magic thumb that grants him great wisdom. He is often shown hunting with his hounds Bran and Sceólang and fighting with spear and sword. His tales make up the Fenian Cycle, much of it told by his son, the poet Oisín.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "fionn-mac-cumhaill-c01",
        "fionn-mac-cumhaill-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Dalam bahasa Irlandia Kuno finn berarti \"putih, terang, elok\". Fionn adalah putra anumerta Cumhall, pemimpin Fianna, dan Muirne; Cumhall dibunuh Goll mac Morna dalam Battle of Cnucha setelah melarikan Muirne. Menurut artikel de, Fionn adalah pahlawan Irlandia dari epos abad pertengahan dan tokoh utama Siklus Finn; ia memiliki \"tangan penyembuh\" sehingga siapa pun yang diberinya air akan sembuh, belajar ilmu bertarung dari bibinya, dan menerima pedang Mac an Luinn dari ibunya. Dalam kisah-kisah kemudian, seperti Diarmuid und Gráinne, menjelang akhir hidupnya ia digambarkan iri dan pendendam.",
      "en": "In Old Irish finn means \"white, bright, fair\". Fionn was the posthumous son of Cumhall, leader of the Fianna, and Muirne; Cumhall was killed by Goll mac Morna at the Battle of Cnucha after carrying Muirne off. According to the de article, Fionn is an Irish hero of medieval epic and the namesake of the Finn Cycle; he has \"healing hands\", so that anyone he gives water to recovers, learns fighting arts from his aunt, and receives the sword Mac an Luinn from his mother. In later tales such as Diarmuid und Gráinne he is shown, near the end of his life, as envious and vengeful.",

        "en": "Fionn has healing hands (heilende Hände); whoever he gives water (Wasser) recovers; he learns combat from his aunt (Tante) Los Lurgann and gets the sword (Schwert) Mac an Luinn from his mother Muirne."

      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam kisah kemudian Fionn pejuang (Krieger) dan peramal (Seher) yang baik, menjelang akhir hidupnya iri (neidisch) dan pendendam (rachsüchtig) dalam Diarmuid und Gráinne.",
        "en": "In later tales Fionn is a good warrior (Krieger) and seer (Seher), late in life envious (neidisch) and vengeful (rachsüchtig) in Diarmuid und Gráinne."

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Manannán mac Lir (\"anak laut\") adalah dewa laut, pejuang, dan raja dunia lain dalam mitologi Gaelik, salah satu Tuatha Dé Danann.",
    "en": "Manannán mac Lir, whose surname means 'son of the Sea', is the Gaelic god of the sea, a warrior and lord of the otherworld, counted among the Tuatha Dé Danann.",
    "claim_ids": [
