  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "barghest-c01",
      "source_id": "barghest-s1",
      "quote": "In Northern English folklore, the Barghest or Barguest is a mythical monstrous black dog with large teeth and claws; however, in other cases, the name can refer to a ghost or household elf, especially in Northumberland and Durham, such as the Cauld Lad of Hylton.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam cerita rakyat Inggris Utara (Northern English folklore), Barghest (Barguest) anjing hitam mengerikan bergigi dan bercakar besar, atau hantu atau elf rumah di Northumberland dan Durham, seperti Cauld Lad of Hylton.",
        "en": "In Northern English folklore the Barghest (Barguest) is a monstrous black dog with large teeth and claws, or a ghost or household elf in Northumberland and Durham, like the Cauld Lad of Hylton."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "barghest-c02",
      "source_id": "barghest-s1",
      "quote": "\"Ghost\" in Northern England was pronounced \"guest\", and the origin is thought to be of the combination burh-ghest, \"town-ghost\". Others explain it as cognate to German Berg-geist, \"mountain ghost\" or Bär-geist, \"bear-ghost\". Another mooted derivation is Bahr-Geist, German for the \"spirit of the funeral bier\".",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ghost di Inggris Utara (Northern England) diucapkan guest, sehingga namanya mungkin burh-ghest; atau dari Berg-geist, Bär-geist, Bahr-Geist (German, Jerman).",
        "en": "Ghost was pronounced guest in Northern England, so the name may be burh-ghest; or from German Berg-geist, Bär-geist or Bahr-Geist."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "barghest-c03",
      "source_id": "barghest-s1",
      "quote": "In Yorkshire, England, one notable case is said to frequent a remote gorge named Troller's Gill in the Yorkshire Dales. A ballad entitled \"The Legend of the Troller's Gill\" can be found in William Hone's Everyday Book (1830).",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di Yorkshire (England), Barghest menghantui ngarai Troller's Gill di Yorkshire Dales; balada The Legend of the Troller's Gill ada dalam Everyday Book karya William Hone (1830).",
        "en": "In Yorkshire, England, a Barghest haunts the gorge of Troller's Gill in the Yorkshire Dales; the ballad The Legend of the Troller's Gill is in William Hone's Everyday Book (1830)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "barghest-c04",
      "source_id": "barghest-s1",
      "quote": "In Durham, during the 1870s a shapeshifting Barghest was said to live near Darlington and was said to take the form of a headless man (who would vanish in flames), a headless lady, a white cat, a rabbit, a dog, or a black dog.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di Durham pada 1870-an Barghest yang berubah wujud tinggal dekat Darlington, menjelma lelaki atau perempuan tanpa kepala, kucing putih, kelinci, atau anjing hitam.",
        "en": "In 1870s Durham a shapeshifting Barghest near Darlington took the form of a headless man or lady, a white cat, rabbit or black dog."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "barghest-c05",
      "source_id": "barghest-s1",
      "quote": "The Barghest often allegedly serves as an omen of death. At the passing of a notable person the Barghest may appear, followed by all the other dogs of the local area in a kind of funeral procession, heralding the person's death with howling and barking.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Barghest pertanda kematian; saat tokoh meninggal ia muncul diikuti anjing-anjing setempat dalam semacam iring-iringan jenazah.",
        "en": "The Barghest is an omen of death; at a notable death it appears followed by the local dogs in a funeral procession."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "barghest-c06",
      "source_id": "barghest-s1",
      "quote": "Besides taking the form of a large black dog with fiery eyes, it may also become invisible and walk about with the sound of rattling chains. It may also foretell the death of an individual by lying across the threshold of his or her house, and like the vampire the Barghest is unable to cross rivers.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Selain anjing hitam bermata api, ia dapat tak terlihat dengan bunyi rantai, meramalkan kematian dengan berbaring di ambang pintu, dan seperti vampir tak dapat menyeberangi sungai.",
        "en": "Besides a fiery-eyed black dog it can go invisible with rattling chains, foretell death lying across a threshold, and like the vampire cannot cross rivers."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "barghest-c07",
      "source_id": "barghest-s2",
      "quote": "Der Barghest (auch „Bargtjest“, „Bo-guest“ oder „Bargest“) ist ein mythischer Schwarzer Hund, an den man im Norden Englands, insbesondere in Yorkshire, glaubte.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Barghest (Bargtjest, Bo-guest, Bargest) adalah anjing hitam mitos (mythischer Schwarzer Hund) yang dipercaya di utara Inggris (Norden Englands), terutama Yorkshire.",
        "en": "The Barghest (Bargtjest, Bo-guest, Bargest) is a mythical black dog (mythischer Schwarzer Hund) believed in northern England (Norden Englands), especially Yorkshire."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "barghest-c08",
      "source_id": "barghest-s2",
      "quote": "Es gibt eine Geschichte, wonach er gelegentlich in die Stadt York eindringt und dann einsamen Reisenden auf den engen Straßen der Stadt auflauert.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ada cerita ia kadang memasuki kota (Stadt) York dan mengintai pelancong sendirian (einsamen Reisenden) di jalan sempit.",
        "en": "A tale says it sometimes enters the city (Stadt) of York to stalk lone travellers (einsamen Reisenden) in narrow streets."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "barghest-c09",
      "source_id": "barghest-s2",
      "quote": "Er soll kein Geist sein, sondern eher ein Kobold, der sich unter anderem in eine Hundeform verwandeln kann.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut sebagian sumber ia bukan hantu (Geist), melainkan kobold (Kobold) yang dapat berubah menjadi anjing (Hundeform).",
        "en": "Some say it is not a ghost (Geist) but a kobold (Kobold) able to turn into a dog (Hundeform)."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Dullahan adalah makhluk legenda cerita rakyat Irlandia, penunggang kuda hitam tanpa kepala atau kusir kereta yang membawa kepalanya sendiri, dan dianggap pertanda kematian.",
    "en": "The Dullahan is a legendary creature of Irish folklore, a headless rider on a black horse or a coachman carrying his own head, seen as an omen of death.",
    "claim_ids": [

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dullahan (bahasa Irlandia: Dubhlachan; dúlachán) adalah makhluk legenda dalam cerita rakyat Irlandia (Irish folklore). Ia digambarkan sebagai penunggang kuda hitam tanpa kepala, atau sebagai kusir kereta, yang membawa kepalanya sendiri di tangan atau di bawah lengan. Menurut leksikografer Edward O'Reilly namanya, yang mengacu pada hobgoblin, secara harfiah berarti \"orang gelap dan murung\"; akar kata dubh berarti \"hitam\". Ia juga disebut Colainn Gan Cheann (\"tanpa kepala\"), dan kendaraannya disebut Cóiste Gan Cheann (Headless Coach, kereta tanpa kepala) atau cóiste bodhar (\"kereta bisu\").",
      "en": "The Dullahan (Irish: Dubhlachan; dúlachán) is a legendary creature of Irish folklore. He rides a black horse, or drives a coach, without a head on his shoulders, holding it instead in his hand or tucked under his arm. According to the lexicographer Edward O'Reilly the name, referring to a hobgoblin, literally means 'dark, sullen person'; the stem dubh means 'black'. He is also called Colainn Gan Cheann ('without a head'), and his vehicle is the Cóiste Gan Cheann ('Headless Coach') or cóiste bodhar ('soundless coach').",
      "claim_ids": [
        "dullahan-c01",
        "dullahan-c02",
        "dullahan-c03",
        "dullahan-c04"

    {
      "id": "Kepalanya yang terpenggal berupa mengerikan: dalam kisah Croker, kepala itu tampak seperti keju krim besar yang dikelilingi puding hitam, pucat abu-abu, dengan dua mata berapi yang berkilat seperti meteor. Menurut artikel de, Dullahan adalah makhluk jahat berwujud penunggang atau kesatria tanpa kepala, biasanya arwah prajurit dan pahlawan yang gugur, bersenjata baju zirah, perisai, dan pedang, dan dalam kebanyakan legenda ditafsirkan sebagai pertanda kematian; biasanya laki-laki, tetapi bisa juga perempuan.",
      "en": "His severed head is hideous: in a tale by Croker it looks like a large cream cheese hung round with black puddings, ashen pale, with two fiery eyes flashing like meteors.       "source_id": "dullahan-s1",
      "quote": "\"Headless Coach\" (Irish: Cóiste Gan Cheann ) or the \"Soundless Coach\" (literally \"deaf coach\", Irish: cóiste bodhar ; Hiberno-English: Coshta Bower, corrupted to \"coach-a-bower\") is the name given to the vehicle driven by the Dullahan.",

      "source_id": "dullahan-s1",
      "quote": "It looked like a large cream cheese hung round with black puddings: no speck of colour enlivened the ashy paleness of the depressed features; the skin lay stretched over the unearthly surface almost like the parchment head of a drum.       "locator": null,

      "statement": {

      "source_id": "dullahan-s2",
      "quote": "Der Begriff Dullahan (im Irischen auch Gan Ceann, zu deutsch „kopflos“) stammt aus der irischen Mythologie und bezeichnet eine Art böses Wesen, welches als kopfloser Reiter oder auch kopfloser Ritter in Erscheinung tritt. Es handelt sich meist um gefallene Soldaten und Helden, die als Geistererscheinung in Form eines kopflosen Reiters weiterleben.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dullahan (Gan Ceann, kopflos) dari mitologi Irlandia (irischen Mythologie) adalah makhluk jahat (böses Wesen), penunggang atau kesatria tanpa kepala (kopfloser Reiter, Ritter), biasanya arwah prajurit (Soldaten) dan pahlawan (Helden) yang gugur.",
        "en": "The Dullahan (Gan Ceann, kopflos) of Irish mythology (irischen Mythologie) is an evil being (böses Wesen), a headless rider or knight (kopfloser Reiter, Ritter), usually fallen soldiers (Soldaten) and heroes (Helden)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "dullahan-c07",
      "source_id": "dullahan-s2",
      "quote": "Mit Rüstung, Schild und Schwert bewaffnet ist der Dullahan in den meisten mythologischen Sagen als Todesomen zu deuten. Dullahan werden meist als männliche Wesen dargestellt, können jedoch auch weiblich sein.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Bersenjata baju zirah (Rüstung), perisai (Schild), dan pedang (Schwert), Dullahan ditafsirkan sebagai pertanda kematian (Todesomen); biasanya laki-laki (männliche), bisa perempuan (weiblich).",
        "en": "Armed with armour (Rüstung), shield (Schild) and sword (Schwert), the Dullahan is read as a death omen (Todesomen); usually male (männliche), sometimes female (weiblich)."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Herne the Hunter adalah hantu cerita rakyat Inggris yang dikaitkan dengan Windsor Forest dan Great Park di Berkshire, bertanduk rusa, menunggang kuda, dan menggemerincingkan rantai.",
    "en": "Herne the Hunter is a ghost of English folklore tied to Windsor Forest and Great Park in Berkshire, antlered, riding a horse and rattling chains.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "herne-the-hunter-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam cerita rakyat Inggris (English folklore), Herne the Hunter adalah hantu yang dikaitkan dengan Windsor Forest dan Great Park di county Berkshire. Ia dikatakan bertanduk rusa yang tumbuh dari kepalanya, menunggang kuda, menyiksa ternak, dan menggemerincingkan rantai. Sebutan tertua tentang Herne berasal dari lakon William Shakespeare The Merry Wives of Windsor (1597), dan tidak dapat diketahui sejauh mana Shakespeare memakai legenda setempat yang nyata.",
      "en": "In English folklore Herne the Hunter is a ghost tied to Windsor Forest and Great Park in the county of Berkshire. He is said to have antlers sprouting from his head, to ride a horse, plague cattle and rattle chains. The oldest mention comes from William Shakespeare's play The Merry Wives of Windsor (1597), and how far Shakespeare drew on a genuine local legend cannot be known.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "herne-the-hunter-c01",
        "herne-the-hunter-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Dalam lakon itu Herne adalah arwah bekas penjaga Windsor Forest yang sepanjang musim dingin, tengah malam, berjalan mengelilingi sebatang pohon ek dengan tanduk besar, merusak pohon itu, membuat sapi perah mengeluarkan darah alih-alih susu, dan menggemerincingkan rantai dengan mengerikan. Kuarto pertama lakon itu (1602) menyebut hantu yang dieja Horne ini diciptakan untuk menakuti anak-anak agar patuh, dalam wujud rusa jantan besar. Menurut artikel de, Herne ditafsirkan sebagai dewa perburuan dan penguasa satwa buruan, mungkin dewa lokal Anglo-Saxon di Windsor Forest; ada yang menyamakannya dengan Cernunnos, dan ada pula yang melihatnya sebagai wujud Odin dalam Wilden Jagd (Perburuan Liar).",
      "en": "In the play Herne is the ghost of a former Windsor Forest keeper who all winter, at midnight, walks round an oak with great ragged horns, blights the tree, makes milk cows yield blood and shakes a chain hideously. The play's first quarto (1602) says this ghost, spelled Horne, was invented to frighten children into obedience, in the form of a great stag. According to the de article, Herne is read as a god of the hunt and lord of game, perhaps a local Anglo-Saxon deity of Windsor Forest; some equate him with Cernunnos, others see in him a form of Odin in the Wilden Jagd, the Wild Hunt.",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "herne-the-hunter-c01",
      "source_id": "herne-the-hunter-s1",
      "quote": "In English folklore, Herne the Hunter is a ghost associated with Windsor Forest and Great Park in the English county of Berkshire. He is said to have antlers growing from his head, ride a horse, torment cattle, and rattle chains.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam cerita rakyat Inggris (English folklore), Herne the Hunter adalah hantu Windsor Forest dan Great Park di Berkshire, bertanduk rusa, menunggang kuda, menyiksa ternak, dan menggemerincingkan rantai.",
        "en": "In English folklore Herne the Hunter is a ghost of Windsor Forest and Great Park in Berkshire, antlered, horse-riding, tormenting cattle and rattling chains."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "herne-the-hunter-c02",
      "source_id": "herne-the-hunter-s1",
      "quote": "The earliest mention of Herne comes from William Shakespeare's 1597 play The Merry Wives of Windsor, and it is impossible to know how accurately or to what degree Shakespeare may have incorporated a real local legend into his work",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebutan tertua Herne dari lakon William Shakespeare The Merry Wives of Windsor (1597); tak diketahui seberapa jauh ia memakai legenda setempat.",
        "en": "Herne is first mentioned in William Shakespeare's 1597 play The Merry Wives of Windsor; how far it used a real local legend is unknown."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "herne-the-hunter-c03",
      "source_id": "herne-the-hunter-s1",
      "quote": "Doth all the winter-time, at still midnight Walk round about an oak, with great ragg'd horns; And there he blasts the tree, and takes the cattle, And makes milch-kine yield blood, and shakes a chain In a most hideous and dreadful manner.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Shakespeare: sepanjang musim dingin, tengah malam, Herne mengelilingi pohon ek dengan tanduk besar, merusak pohon, membuat sapi perah (milch-kine) mengeluarkan darah, dan menggemerincingkan rantai.",
        "en": "Shakespeare: all winter at midnight Herne walks round an oak with great ragged horns, blasts the tree, makes milch-kine yield blood and shakes a chain."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "herne-the-hunter-c04",
      "source_id": "herne-the-hunter-s1",
      "quote": "The first quarto of the play from 1602 includes a different version of this text, which states that the ghost (spelled \"Horne\" in this version) was invented to scare children into obedience, and that mothers tell their children the tale of a ghost who walks the forest in the form of a great stag.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kuarto pertama (1602) menyebut hantu Horne diciptakan untuk menakuti anak-anak agar patuh, berwujud rusa jantan besar.",
        "en": "The 1602 first quarto says the ghost, Horne, was invented to scare children into obedience, walking the forest as a great stag."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "herne-the-hunter-c05",
      "source_id": "herne-the-hunter-s1",
      "quote": "Officially published versions of the play refer only to the tale of Herne as the ghost of a former Windsor Forest keeper who haunts a particular oak tree at midnight in the winter time.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Versi resmi lakon menyebut Herne arwah bekas penjaga Windsor Forest yang menghantui pohon ek pada tengah malam di musim dingin.",
        "en": "Official versions call Herne the ghost of a former Windsor Forest keeper haunting an oak at midnight in winter."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "herne-the-hunter-c06",
      "source_id": "herne-the-hunter-s2",
      "quote": "Herne wird als Gott der Jagd und Herr des Wildes gedeutet. Vermutungen zufolge war Herne eine Lokalgottheit der Angelsachsen im Windsor Forest, einem Wald, der sich über die heutigen Grafschaften Berkshire, Buckinghamshire und Hampshire erstreckte. Man nimmt an, Herne sei identisch mit Cernunnos",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Herne ditafsirkan sebagai dewa perburuan (Gott der Jagd) dan penguasa satwa (Herr des Wildes), mungkin dewa lokal Anglo-Saxon (Angelsachsen) di Windsor Forest; ada yang menyamakannya dengan Cernunnos.",
        "en": "Herne is read as a god of the hunt (Gott der Jagd) and lord of game (Herr des Wildes), perhaps a local Anglo-Saxon (Angelsachsen) god of Windsor Forest; some equate him with Cernunnos."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "herne-the-hunter-c07",
      "source_id": "herne-the-hunter-s2",
      "quote": "Konträr dazu sieht man in Herne eine Erscheinungsform des germanischen Gottes Odin bei der Wilden Jagd.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebaliknya, ada yang melihat Herne sebagai wujud dewa Jermanik (germanischen Gottes) Odin dalam Perburuan Liar (Wilden Jagd).",
        "en": "Others see Herne as a form of the Germanic god (germanischen Gottes) Odin in the Wild Hunt (Wilden Jagd)."
      }

  "traits": [],
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Nodens adalah dewa penyembuh Kelt yang dipuja di Britania kuno, terutama di tempat suci Lydney Park, dan dikaitkan dengan anjing.",
    "en": "Nodens is a Celtic healing god worshipped in ancient Britain, chiefly at the shrine of Lydney Park, and linked with dogs.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "nodens-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "*Nodens, *Nodons, atau *Nudens (direkonstruksi dari bentuk datif Nodenti atau Nodonti) adalah dewa penyembuh Kelt (Celtic) yang dipuja di Britania kuno. Tidak ada gambar dirinya yang bertahan, tetapi plakat persembahan dari tempat suci di Lydney Park (Gloucestershire) menunjukkan kaitannya dengan anjing, hewan yang pada zaman kuno melambangkan penyembuhan. Dewa ini hanya dikenal di satu tempat lain, Cockersand Moss (Lancashire). Dalam kebanyakan prasasti ia disamakan dengan Mars sebagai penyembuh, bukan pejuang, dan namanya sekerabat dengan tokoh mitologi Kelt kemudian seperti Nuada dari Irlandia dan Nudd dari Wales.",
      "en": "Known as Nodens, Nodons or Nudens, a name rebuilt from the dative forms Nodenti and Nodonti, this is a Celtic god of healing in ancient Britain. No image of him survives, but votive plaques from a shrine at Lydney Park (Gloucestershire) show a link with dogs, beasts tied to healing in antiquity. He is known from just one other place, Cockersand Moss (Lancashire). Most inscriptions equate him with Mars as healer rather than warrior, and his name is related to later Celtic figures such as the Irish Nuada and the Welsh Nudd.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "nodens-c01",
        "nodens-c02",
        "nodens-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Filolog dan penulis J. R. R. Tolkien diundang menyelidiki prasasti Latin di Lydney Park, dan para sarjana mencatat beberapa kemungkinan pengaruhnya pada tulisan Middle-earth, termasuk Celebrimbor sang pandai besi pembuat Rings of Power, yang namanya, seperti julukan Nuada Airgetlám, berarti \"Tangan Perak\". Nodens juga muncul dalam karya Arthur Machen dan Cthulhu Mythos H. P. Lovecraft. Menurut artikel de, di Lydney Park ditemukan patung perunggu kereta yang ditarik empat kuda, monumen seorang lelaki membunuh ikan bertuliskan nama Nodons, dan persembahan lain bernama Nodons.",
      "en": "The philologist and author J. R. R. Tolkien was asked to study the Latin inscription at Lydney Park, and scholars note likely influences on his Middle-earth writings, including the Elvish smith Celebrimbor, maker of the Rings of Power, whose name, like Nuada's epithet Airgetlám, means 'Silver-hand'. Nodens also appears in the works of Arthur Machen and in H. P. Lovecraft's Cthulhu Mythos. According to the de article, Lydney Park yielded a bronze of a chariot drawn by four horses, a monument of a man killing a fish inscribed with the name Nodons, and other offerings naming Nodons.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "nodens-c04",
        "nodens-c05",
        "nodens-c06",
        "nodens-c07"
      ]
    }
  ],

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "nodens-c01",
      "source_id": "nodens-s1",
      "quote": "(reconstructed from the dative Nodenti or Nodonti) is a Celtic healing god worshipped in Ancient Britain. Although no physical depiction of him has survived, votive plaques found in a shrine at Lydney Park (Gloucestershire) indicate his connection with dogs, a beast associated with healing symbolism in antiquity.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nodens (dari datif Nodenti atau Nodonti) dewa penyembuh Kelt (Celtic) Britania kuno (Ancient Britain); plakat di Lydney Park (Gloucestershire) mengaitkannya dengan anjing.",
        "en": "Nodens (from dative Nodenti or Nodonti) is a Celtic healing god of Ancient Britain; plaques at Lydney Park (Gloucestershire) link him with dogs."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nodens-c02",
      "source_id": "nodens-s1",
      "quote": "The deity is known in only one other location, in Cockersand Moss (Lancashire).",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dewa ini hanya dikenal di satu tempat lain, Cockersand Moss (Lancashire).",
        "en": "The deity is known at only one other place, Cockersand Moss (Lancashire)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nodens-c03",
      "source_id": "nodens-s1",
      "quote": "He was equated on most inscriptions with the Roman god Mars (as a healer rather than as a warrior) and associated in a curse with Silvanus (a hunting-god). His name is cognate with that of later Celtic mythological figures, such as the Irish Nuada and the Welsh Nudd.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia disamakan dengan Mars (Roman) sebagai penyembuh; namanya sekerabat dengan Nuada (Irish, Irlandia) dan Nudd (Welsh, Wales).",
        "en": "He was equated with the Roman Mars as healer; his name is cognate with the Irish Nuada and Welsh Nudd."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nodens-c04",
      "source_id": "nodens-s1",
      "quote": "The philologist and author J. R. R. Tolkien was invited to investigate the Latin inscription, and scholars have noted several likely influences on his Middle-earth fantasy writings, including the Elvish smith, maker of Rings of Power, Celebrimbor, whose name, like that of Nuada's epithet Airgetlám, means 'Silver-hand'.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "J. R. R. Tolkien menyelidiki prasasti Latin (Latin) itu; mungkin memengaruhi Middle-earth dan Celebrimbor, yang namanya seperti Airgetlám berarti Tangan Perak (Silver-hand).",
        "en": "J. R. R. Tolkien investigated the Latin inscription; it may have shaped Middle-earth and Celebrimbor, whose name like Airgetlám means Silver-hand."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nodens-c05",
      "source_id": "nodens-s1",
      "quote": "Nodens appears, too, in the works of Arthur Machen, as well as H. P. Lovecraft's Cthulhu Mythos.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nodens muncul dalam karya Arthur Machen dan Cthulhu Mythos H. P. Lovecraft.",
        "en": "Nodens appears in Arthur Machen's works and H. P. Lovecraft's Cthulhu Mythos."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nodens-c06",
      "source_id": "nodens-s2",
      "quote": "Nodons, auch Nodens, ist ein in Britannien verehrter altkeltischer Gott. Er wird als Gott der Heilung, des Wassers und der Hunde beschrieben.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nodons (Nodens) dewa Kelt kuno (altkeltischer Gott) di Britania (Britannien), dewa penyembuhan (Heilung), air (Wassers), dan anjing (Hunde).",
        "en": "Nodons (Nodens) is an old Celtic god (altkeltischer Gott) of Britain (Britannien), god of healing (Heilung), water (Wassers) and dogs (Hunde)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nodens-c07",
      "source_id": "nodens-s2",
      "quote": "Neben Votivgaben, die den Namen Nodons tragen, wurde eine Bronzestatue gefunden, die einen von vier Pferden gezogenen Wagen darstellt, auf dem ein Mann mit einer Keule steht, sowie ein Monument, auf dem ein Mann einen Fisch tötet.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di Lydney Park ditemukan persembahan bernama Nodons, patung perunggu (Bronzestatue) kereta empat kuda (vier Pferden), dan monumen lelaki membunuh ikan (Fisch).",
        "en": "At Lydney Park were found offerings naming Nodons, a bronze (Bronzestatue) of a four-horse (vier Pferden) chariot, and a monument of a man killing a fish (Fisch)."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Sulis adalah dewi Kelt yang dipuja di mata air panas Bath, Britania, dan oleh orang Romawi-Britania disebut Sulis Minerva.",
    "en": "Sulis is a Celtic goddess worshipped at the hot spring of Bath in Britain, called Sulis Minerva by the Romano-British.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "sulis-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam politeisme Kelt (Celtic) setempat di Britania Raya (Great Britain), Sulis adalah dewi yang dipuja di mata air panas Bath. Orang Romawi-Britania (Romano-British) memujanya sebagai Sulis Minerva; benda persembahan dan lempeng timah bertulisan menunjukkan bahwa ia dipandang sebagai dewi ibu yang memberi hidup sekaligus sebagai pelaksana kutukan yang diminta pemujanya. Makna namanya masih diperdebatkan, tetapi banyak ahli bahasa menganggapnya sekerabat dengan kata Irlandia Kuno (Old Irish) súil, \"mata, penglihatan\". Menurut artikel de, Sulis adalah dewi matahari (Sonnen) dan penyembuhan (Heil-Gottheit) bangsa Kelt Britania yang disamakan dengan Minerva.",
      "en": "In the local Celtic polytheism of Great Britain, Sulis was the goddess worshipped at Bath's thermal spring. The Romano-British honoured her as Sulis Minerva; offerings and inscribed lead tablets show her seen both as a life-giving mother goddess and as an agent of curses her worshippers called down. The meaning of her name is debated, but many linguists tie it to Old Irish súil, 'eye, sight'. According to the de article she was a sun (Sonnen) and healing (Heil-Gottheit) goddess of the British Celts, equated with Minerva.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "sulis-c01",
        "sulis-c02",
        "sulis-c06"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Bentuk Sul adalah bacaan tradisional, tetapi tahun 1979 ditemukan lempeng kutukan timah di mata air suci Aquae Sulis yang memuat frasa \"dea Sulis\", satu-satunya contoh nama itu dalam kasus nominatif. Orang Romawi menamai tempat itu Aquae Sulis, \"perairan Sulis\"; ia kemungkinan dipuja sebagai penyembuh yang mata air panasnya dapat menyembuhkan penyakit, dan menurut Miranda Green pemujaannya di Bath aktif hingga pertengahan abad keempat Masehi. Di mata air itu ditemukan lebih dari 12.500 koin Romawi dan 18 koin Kelt, serta sekitar 130 lempeng kutukan yang kebanyakan ditujukan kepada Sulis.",
      "en": "Sul is the traditional reading, but in 1979 a lead curse tablet from the sacred spring of Aquae Sulis was found bearing the words 'dea Sulis', the only instance of the name in the nominative. The Romans called the place Aquae Sulis, 'the waters of Sulis'; she was probably honoured as a healer whose hot springs could cure illness, and according to Miranda Green her cult at Bath lasted until the mid-fourth century CE. Over 12,500 Roman and 18 Celtic coins were recovered from the spring, along with about 130 curse tablets, most addressed to Sulis.",

        "sulis-c03",
        "sulis-c04",
        "sulis-c05",
        "sulis-c07",

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Boann adalah dewi Irlandia untuk Sungai Boyne, ibu Aengus dari hubungannya dengan Dagda, yang menurut legenda menciptakan sungai itu lalu tenggelam di dalamnya.",
    "en": "Boann is the Irish goddess of the River Boyne, mother of Aengus by the Dagda, who in legend created the river and drowned in it.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "boann-c01",
      "boann-c04",
      "boann-c05"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Boann atau Boand adalah dewi Irlandia (Irish) untuk Sungai Boyne (Bóinn) di provinsi bersejarah Meath. Menurut Lebor Gabála Érenn dan Táin Bó Fraích ia saudari Befind dan putri Delbáeth, putra Elada, dari Tuatha Dé Danann; suaminya disebut Nechtan atau Elcmar, dan dari kekasihnya, Dagda, ia melahirkan Aengus. Dalam dinsenchas namanya ditafsirkan sebagai \"sapi putih\". Untuk menyembunyikan kehamilannya dari Elcmar, Dagda membuat \"matahari berhenti\" sehingga sembilan bulan berlalu seperti sehari.",
      "en": "Boann, also spelled Boand, is the Irish deity of the River Boyne (Bóinn), which flows through the historic province of Meath. In the Lebor Gabála Érenn and Táin Bó Fraích she is a sister of Befind, and her father is Delbáeth, whose own father was Elada of the Tuatha Dé Danann; her husband is given as Nechtan or Elcmar, and by her lover the Dagda she bore Aengus. The dinsenchas read her name as 'white cow'. To hide the pregnancy from Elcmar, the Dagda made 'the sun stand still', so nine months passed like a single day.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "boann-c01",
        "boann-c02",
        "boann-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Menurut Dindsenchas, Boann menciptakan Sungai Boyne: meski dilarang suaminya, Nechtan, ia mendatangi Well of Segais yang dikelilingi sembilan pohon hazel ajaib dan menantang kekuatannya dengan berjalan mengitarinya tuathal (melawan arah matahari). Airnya meluap dan menerjang ke laut sehingga terbentuk Sungai Boyne, dan Boann kehilangan lengan, kaki, dan mata, lalu nyawanya. Artikel de juga mencatat bahwa daerah di sekitar makam prasejarah Newgrange di lembah Boyne dinamai menurut dia, Brug na Boinne.",
      "en": "According to the Dindsenchas, Boann made the Boyne: though her husband Nechtan forbade it, she went to the Well of Segais, ringed by nine magic hazels, and defied it by circling it tuathal, against the sun. Its waters burst out and raced to the sea, forming the Boyne, and Boann lost an arm, a leg and an eye, then her life. The de article adds that the land around the prehistoric tombs of Newgrange in the Boyne valley is named Brug na Boinne after her.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "boann-c04",
        "boann-c05",
        "boann-c06",
        "boann-c07"
      ]
    }
  ],

      "id": "boann-c01",
      "source_id": "boann-s1",
      "quote": "Boann or Boand is the Irish goddess of the River Boyne (Bóinn), an important river in Ireland's historical province of Meath. According to the Lebor Gabála Érenn and Táin Bó Fraích she was the sister of Befind and daughter of Delbáeth, son of Elada, of the Tuatha Dé Danann. Her husband is variously Nechtan or Elcmar.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Boann (Boand) dewi Irlandia (Irish) Sungai Boyne (River Boyne, Bóinn) di Meath; menurut Lebor Gabála Érenn dan Táin Bó Fraích saudari Befind, putri Delbáeth putra Elada dari Tuatha Dé Danann; suaminya Nechtan atau Elcmar; ibu Aengus dengan Dagda (the Dagda); di Irlandia (Ireland).",
        "en": "Boann (Boand) is the Irish goddess of the River Boyne (Bóinn) in Meath; per the Lebor Gabála Érenn and Táin Bó Fraích, sister of Befind, daughter of Delbáeth son of Elada of the Tuatha Dé Danann; husband Nechtan or Elcmar; mother of Aengus by the Dagda."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "boann-c02",
      "source_id": "boann-s1",
      "quote": "Her name is interpreted as \"white cow\" (Irish: bó fhionn ; Old Irish: bó find ) in the dinsenchas, where she is also called \"White Boand\".",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam dinsenchas namanya ditafsirkan sebagai sapi putih (white cow).",
        "en": "In the dinsenchas her name is read as white cow."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "boann-c03",
      "source_id": "boann-s1",
      "quote": "To hide the pregnancy from Elcmar, the Dagda casts a spell on him, making \"the sun stand still\" so he will not notice the passing of time. Meanwhile, nine months pass and Boann gives birth to Aengus.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dagda membuat matahari berhenti agar Elcmar tak sadar; sembilan bulan berlalu dan Boann melahirkan Aengus, di Brú na Bóinne.",
        "en": "The Dagda made the sun stand still so Elcmar would not notice; nine months passed and Boann bore Aengus, at Brú na Bóinne."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "boann-c04",
      "source_id": "boann-s1",
      "quote": "Though forbidden to by her husband, Nechtan, Boann approached the magical Well of Segais (also known as the Connla's Well), which was surrounded, according to the legend, by nine magic hazel-trees.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut Dindsenchas, meski dilarang Nechtan, Boann mendatangi Well of Segais (Connla's Well) yang dikelilingi sembilan pohon hazel ajaib.",
        "en": "Per the Dindsenchas, though Nechtan forbade it, Boann approached the Well of Segais (Connla's Well), ringed by nine magic hazels."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "boann-c05",
      "source_id": "boann-s1",
      "quote": "Boann challenged the power of the well by walking around it tuathal; this caused the waters to surge up violently and rush down to the sea, creating the Boyne. In this catastrophe, she was swept along in the rushing waters, and lost an arm, leg and eye, and ultimately her life, in the flood.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Boann mengitari sumur itu tuathal; air meluap ke laut menciptakan Boyne, dan ia kehilangan lengan, kaki, mata, lalu nyawanya.",
        "en": "Boann circled the well tuathal; the waters surged to the sea creating the Boyne, and she lost an arm, leg, eye and then her life."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "boann-c06",
      "source_id": "boann-s2",
      "quote": "ist eine Figur der keltischen Mythologie Irlands und dort die Flussgöttin des Boyne. Die Mythologie erzählt, sie habe die geheime Quelle ihres Gatten Nechtan im Síd durch Herumgehen gegen den Sonnenlauf (tuaithbel) entweiht, worauf sie darin ertrunken sei und der Boyne entstand.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Bóann tokoh mitologi Kelt Irlandia (Irlands), dewi sungai (Flussgöttin) Boyne; ia menodai mata air rahasia Nechtan di Síd dengan berjalan melawan matahari (tuaithbel), tenggelam, dan Boyne pun tercipta.",
        "en": "Bóann is a figure of Irish Celtic myth, river goddess (Flussgöttin) of the Boyne; she profaned Nechtan's secret spring in the Síd by walking against the sun (tuaithbel), drowned, and the Boyne arose."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "boann-c07",
      "source_id": "boann-s2",
      "quote": "Die Gegend um die vorkeltischen Grabhügel bei Newgrange im Boyne-Tal wird nach ihr Brug na Boinne genannt.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Daerah sekitar makam prasejarah (vorkeltischen Grabhügel) Newgrange di lembah Boyne (Boyne-Tal) dinamai Brug na Boinne menurut dia.",
        "en": "The land around the pre-Celtic mounds of Newgrange in the Boyne valley (Boyne-Tal) is called Brug na Boinne after her."
      }
    }

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Hobgoblin adalah roh rumah tangga dalam cerita rakyat Inggris, dahulu dianggap penolong tetapi sejak penyebaran Kristen sering dianggap jahil.",
    "en": "The hobgoblin is a household spirit of English folklore, once thought helpful but since the spread of Christianity often seen as mischievous.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "hobgoblin-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Hobgoblin adalah roh rumah tangga dalam cerita rakyat Inggris (English folklore) yang dahulu dianggap suka menolong, tetapi sejak Kristen (Christianity) menyebar sering dipandang jahil. Shakespeare menyebut tokoh Puck dalam A Midsummer Night's Dream sebagai hobgoblin. Istilah ini berasal dari kata hob dan tercatat sekitar 1530. Menurut artikel fr, hobgoblin adalah makhluk legenda Britania (britannique) yang ramah dan kadang suka menolong, tinggal di rumah-rumah pedesaan; dalam cerita rakyat Inggris yang lebih kemudian ia kadang digambarkan sebagai roh jahat dan dicampuradukkan dengan goblin.",
      "en": "The hobgoblin is a household spirit of English folklore, once thought of as helpful but, after Christianity spread, often treated as a nuisance. Shakespeare names Puck in A Midsummer Night's Dream as a hobgoblin. The word grows out of hob and is recorded from around 1530. According to the fr article, the hobgoblin is a legendary British (britannique) creature, friendly and sometimes helpful, living in country homes; in later English folklore it is at times pictured as an evil spirit and confused with the goblin.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "hobgoblin-c01",
        "hobgoblin-c02",
        "hobgoblin-c07"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Hobgoblin tampaknya lelaki kecil berbulu yang, seperti kerabat dekatnya brownie, sering tinggal di rumah manusia dan mengerjakan tugas kecil seperti membersihkan debu dan menyetrika ketika keluarga tidur, cukup dengan imbalan makanan. Mereka lebih gemar bercanda daripada brownie, mudah tersinggung, dan bisa jahil, menakutkan, bahkan berbahaya; memberi mereka pakaian sering membuat mereka pergi untuk selamanya. Di Lancashire dan Yorkshire, menurut folklorist Elizabeth Mary Wright, hobgoblin juga disebut dobby. Menurut artikel fr, nama Hob mungkin berasal dari kata Wales (gallois) hob \"melompat\" atau kata Inggris hob \"perapian\", dan Hob juga nama kecil Robert/Robin yang mungkin mengaitkannya dengan Robin Goodfellow (Puck).",
      "en": "Hobgoblins seem to be small, hairy men who, like their close kin the brownies, often live in human houses and do small chores such as dusting and ironing while the family sleeps, usually paid only in food. They are fonder of pranks than brownies, quick to take offence and can be mischievous, frightening, even dangerous; giving them clothes often drives them away for good. In Lancashire and Yorkshire, according to folklorist Elizabeth Mary Wright, a hobgoblin is also called a dobby. According to the fr article, the name Hob may come from Welsh (gallois) hob 'to hop' or English hob 'hearth', and Hob is also a pet form of Robert/Robin, perhaps tying the hobgoblin to Robin Goodfellow (Puck).",

    },
    {
      "id": "hobgoblin-c04",
      "source_id": "hobgoblin-s1",
      "quote": "While brownies are more peaceful creatures, hobgoblins are more fond of practical jokes. They also seem to be able to shapeshift, as seen in one of Puck's monologues in A Midsummer Night's Dream.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Hobgoblin lebih gemar bercanda daripada brownie dan tampaknya bisa berubah wujud, seperti monolog Puck.",
        "en": "Hobgoblins like pranks more than brownies and seem able to shapeshift, as in a monologue by Puck."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hobgoblin-c05",
      "source_id": "hobgoblin-s1",
      "quote": "Like other fairy folk, hobgoblins are easily annoyed. They can be mischievous, frightening, and even dangerous. Attempts to give them clothing will often banish them forever",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Hobgoblin mudah tersinggung, bisa jahil, menakutkan, berbahaya; diberi pakaian membuat mereka pergi selamanya.",
        "en": "Hobgoblins are easily annoyed and can be dangerous; giving them clothing often banishes them forever."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hobgoblin-c06",
      "source_id": "hobgoblin-s1",
      "quote": "Dobby is another term for hobgoblin in Lancashire and Yorkshire according to the folklorist Elizabeth Mary Wright, especially one that is a relentless prankster.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut Elizabeth Mary Wright, dobby sebutan lain hobgoblin di Lancashire dan Yorkshire.",
        "en": "According to Elizabeth Mary Wright, dobby is another term for hobgoblin in Lancashire and Yorkshire."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hobgoblin-c07",
      "source_id": "hobgoblin-s2",
      "quote": "Le hobgoblin est une créature légendaire du folklore britannique. Il est décrit comme amical et parfois serviable, vivant dans les habitations des campagnes. Dans le folklore anglais plus tardif, il est parfois décrit comme un esprit diabolique et confondu avec le gobelin.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Hobgoblin makhluk legenda Britania (britannique), ramah, tinggal di rumah pedesaan; kemudian kadang dianggap roh jahat dan dicampur dengan goblin (gobelin).",
        "en": "The hobgoblin is a legendary British (britannique) creature, friendly, living in country homes; later sometimes an evil spirit confused with the goblin (gobelin)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hobgoblin-c08",
      "source_id": "hobgoblin-s2",
      "quote": "Une hypothèse dérive hobgoblin du gallois hob, « sautiller », et coblyn « gobelin » ; ce « gobelin sautillant » pouvant être rattaché au personnage légendaire de Puck. Ou bien Hob dérive de l'anglais hob, « foyer »",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Hob mungkin dari kata Wales (gallois) hob melompat, atau kata Inggris (anglais) hob perapian.",
        "en": "Hob may come from Welsh (gallois) hob to hop, or English (anglais) hob hearth."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hobgoblin-c09",
      "source_id": "hobgoblin-s2",
      "quote": "De même Hob est le diminutif de Robert/Robin, rattachant peut-être le hobgoblin au Robin Goodfellow (Puck).",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Hob nama kecil Robert/Robin, mungkin mengaitkan hobgoblin dengan Robin Goodfellow (Puck).",
        "en": "Hob is a diminutive of Robert/Robin, perhaps linking the hobgoblin to Robin Goodfellow (Puck)."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Each-uisge (\"kuda air\") adalah roh air dalam cerita rakyat Irlandia dan Skotlandia, biasanya berwujud kuda, mirip kelpie tetapi jauh lebih ganas.",
    "en": "The each-uisge ('water horse') is a water spirit of Irish and Scottish folklore, usually horse-shaped, like the kelpie but far more vicious.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "each-uisge-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Each-uisge (Gaelik Skotlandia/Scottish Gaelic, harfiah \"kuda air\") adalah roh air dalam cerita rakyat Irlandia (Irish) dan Skotlandia (Scottish), dieja each-uisce (dianglisisasi aughisky atau ech-ushkya) di Irlandia (Ireland) dan cabbyl-ushtey di Isle of Man. Biasanya berwujud kuda, mirip kelpie tetapi jauh lebih ganas. Folklorist Katharine Briggs menyebutnya mungkin yang paling ganas dan berbahaya di antara semua kuda air. Berbeda dengan kelpie yang menghuni sungai, each-uisge tinggal di laut dan danau (loch) air asin maupun tawar di Dataran Tinggi Skotlandia (Scottish Highlands).",
      "en": "The each-uisge (Scottish Gaelic, literally 'water horse') is a water spirit of Irish and Scottish folklore, known on the Isle of Man as cabbyl-ushtey and in Ireland as each-uisce, anglicised as aughisky or ech-ushkya. Most often in horse shape, it resembles the kelpie but is much more savage. The folklorist Katharine Briggs judged it perhaps the fiercest and deadliest of all water-horses. Unlike the kelpie, which keeps to streams and rivers, it lives in the sea and in salt and freshwater lochs of the Scottish Highlands.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "each-uisge-c01",
        "each-uisge-c02",
        "each-uisge-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Each-uisge berubah wujud menjadi kuda atau kuda poni yang bagus, lelaki tampan, atau burung raksasa. Bila seseorang menungganginya, ia hanya aman selama berada di daratan; begitu makhluk itu melihat atau mencium air, kulitnya menjadi lengket dan ia membawa korbannya ke bagian danau yang paling dalam, lalu mencabik dan memakan seluruh tubuhnya kecuali hati, yang mengapung ke permukaan. Dalam wujud manusia ia hanya dapat dikenali dari rumput air atau pasir dan lumpur di rambutnya. Menurut artikel de, each-uisge dianggap salah satu monster air paling berbahaya dalam dunia legenda Kelt Skotlandia (schottisch-keltischen Sagenwelt).",
      "en": "The each-uisge changes into a fine horse or pony, a handsome man or a huge bird. A rider is safe only while it stays inland; once it sees or smells water its skin grows sticky and it carries the victim to the deepest part of the loch, then rips the body apart and eats all of it except the liver, which floats up. In human shape it can be known only by water weed or sand and mud in its hair. According to the de article, it counts among the most dangerous water monsters of the Scottish-Celtic legend world (schottisch-keltischen Sagenwelt).",
      "claim_ids": [

      "quote": "literally \"water horse\") is a water spirit in Irish and Scottish folklore, spelled as the each-uisce (anglicized as aughisky or ech-ushkya) in Ireland and cabbyl-ushtey on the Isle of Man. It usually takes the form of a horse, and is similar to the kelpie but far more vicious.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Each-uisge (Gaelik Skotlandia, Scottish Gaelic) berarti kuda air; roh air Irlandia (Irish) dan Skotlandia (Scottish), each-uisce/aughisky/ech-ushkya di Irlandia (Ireland), cabbyl-ushtey di Isle of Man; berwujud kuda, mirip kelpie tetapi lebih ganas.",
        "en": "Each-uisge (Scottish Gaelic) means water horse; an Irish and Scottish water spirit, each-uisce/aughisky/ech-ushkya in Ireland, cabbyl-ushtey on the Isle of Man; horse-shaped, like the kelpie but more vicious."

      "source_id": "each-uisge-s1",
      "quote": "has been described as \"perhaps the fiercest and most dangerous of all the water-horses\" by the folklorist Katharine Briggs.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {

      "id": "each-uisge-c03",
      "source_id": "each-uisge-s1",
      "quote": "the each-uisge lives in the sea, sea lochs, and fresh water lochs. The each-uisge is a shape-shifter, disguising itself as a fine horse, pony, a handsome man or an enormous bird such as a boobrie.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Each-uisge tinggal di laut dan loch air asin dan tawar di Dataran Tinggi Skotlandia (Scottish Highlands), dan berubah wujud jadi kuda, poni, lelaki tampan, atau burung raksasa.",

    {
      "id": "each-uisge-c04",
      "source_id": "each-uisge-s1",
      "quote": "the each-uisge's skin becomes adhesive and the creature immediately goes to the deepest part of the loch with its victim. After the victim has drowned, the each-uisge tears them apart and devours the entire body except for the liver, which floats to the surface.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kulitnya jadi lengket, membawa korban ke bagian loch terdalam, memakan semuanya kecuali hati.",
        "en": "Its skin turns adhesive; it drags the victim to the deepest loch and eats all but the liver."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "each-uisge-c05",
      "source_id": "each-uisge-s1",
      "quote": "In its human form it is said to appear as a handsome man, and can be recognised as a mythological creature only by the water weeds or profuse sand and mud in its hair.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam wujud manusia dikenali dari rumput air atau pasir dan lumpur di rambutnya.",
        "en": "In human form it is known by water weeds or sand and mud in its hair."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "each-uisge-c06",
      "source_id": "each-uisge-s2",
      "quote": "ist im schottischen Volksglauben eine gestaltwandelnde Kreatur des Feenreiches oder der Anderswelt, die der Sage nach die Lochs und Küstengewässer Schottlands bewohnen soll und als eines der gefährlichsten Wassermonster der schottisch-keltischen Sagenwelt gilt.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Each Uisge makhluk pengubah wujud dari dunia peri (Feenreiches) atau Dunia Lain (Anderswelt), menghuni loch (Lochs) dan perairan pantai Skotlandia (Schottlands), salah satu monster air paling berbahaya di dunia legenda Kelt Skotlandia (schottisch-keltischen Sagenwelt).",
        "en": "Each Uisge is a shapeshifting creature of the fairy realm (Feenreiches) or Otherworld (Anderswelt), living in the lochs (Lochs) and coasts of Scotland (Schottlands), one of the most dangerous water monsters of the Scottish-Celtic legend world (schottisch-keltischen Sagenwelt)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "each-uisge-c07",
      "source_id": "each-uisge-s2",
      "quote": "Das Each Uisge kann leicht mit dem irischen Aughisky verwechselt werden, dem die Erzählungen dieselben Fähigkeiten und eine ähnliche Verhaltensweise zusprechen, das aber die stehenden Gewässer Irlands bewohnt haben soll.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Each Uisge mudah tertukar dengan Aughisky Irlandia (irischen, Irlands) yang berkemampuan sama tetapi menghuni perairan tergenang.",
        "en": "Each Uisge is easily confused with the Irish (irischen, Irlands) Aughisky, with the same powers but living in still waters."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "each-uisge-c08",
      "source_id": "each-uisge-s1",
      "quote": "In revenge the blacksmith and his son made a set of large hooks, in a forge they set up by the loch side. They then roasted a sheep and heated the hooks until they were red hot.",

      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pandai besi dari Raasay membalas dendam dengan kait besar membara dan umpan domba panggang.",
        "en": "A blacksmith from Raasay took revenge with large red-hot hooks and a roasted sheep as bait."
      }
    }

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Huldufólk (\"orang tersembunyi\") adalah makhluk gaib cerita rakyat Islandia dan Faroe yang mirip manusia tetapi hidup di dunia sejajar dan dapat menampakkan diri sesuka hati.",
    "en": "The huldufólk ('hidden folk') are supernatural beings of Icelandic and Faroese folklore who resemble humans but live in a parallel world and can show themselves at will.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "huldufolk-c01",
      "huldufolk-c02"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam cerita rakyat Nordik (Nordic folklore), termasuk Northern Isles, hidden-folk (Faroe dan Islandia/Faroese and Icelandic: huldufólk; Norwegia/Norwegian: huldrefolk) adalah sebutan kolektif untuk kelompok longgar elf, wight, brownie (nisse), troll, dan sejenisnya. Mereka makhluk gaib yang hidup di alam, rupa dan perilakunya mirip manusia tetapi tinggal di dunia sejajar, dan dapat menampakkan diri sesuka hati. Konrad von Maurer mengutip sumber Islandia abad ke-19 bahwa satu-satunya beda yang terlihat adalah philtrum (lekuk di bawah hidung) mereka yang cembung, bukan cekung.",
      "en": "In Nordic folklore, the Northern Isles included, the hidden-folk (Faroese and Icelandic huldufólk; Norwegian huldrefolk) is a group name for a loose gathering of elves, wights, brownies (nisse), trolls and the like. These beings live in nature, look and act much like people yet dwell in a parallel world, and can become visible when they choose. Konrad von Maurer cites a 19th-century Icelandic source saying the only visible difference is a convex rather than concave philtrum beneath the nose.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "huldufolk-c01",
        "huldufolk-c02",
        "huldufolk-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Dalam dongeng Faroe (Faroese) mereka bertubuh besar, berpakaian serba kelabu, berambut hitam, dan tinggal di gundukan; sebagian dongeng Islandia memperingatkan agar tidak melempar batu karena bisa mengenai mereka. Pada abad ke-19 istilah huldufólk dipakai sebagai sinonim álfar (elf); menurut Jón Árnason keduanya sama kecuali álfar bernada merendahkan. Menurut artikel fr, Huldufólk berarti \"orang tersembunyi\" (peuple caché) dan terdiri atas elf, troll, dan orang tak kasat mata yang terutama tinggal di gunung dan batu tempat mereka membangun kota, Álagablettur; jalan di Kópavogur konon dibelokkan agar tidak merusak tempat tinggal mereka.",
      "en": "In Faroese tales they are large, dressed all in grey, black-haired and living in mounds; some Icelandic tales warn against throwing stones lest they hit the hidden people. In the 19th century huldufólk was used as a synonym of álfar (elves); Jón Árnason found the words equivalent except that álfar was disparaging. According to the fr article, Huldufólk means 'hidden people' (peuple caché) and covers elves, trolls and invisible people who live mostly in mountains and rocks where they build their towns, the Álagablettur; a road at Kópavogur was reportedly routed to avoid destroying their home.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "huldufolk-c04",
        "huldufolk-c05",
        "huldufolk-c06",
        "huldufolk-c07"
      ]
    }

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Salmon of Knowledge (An Bradán Feasa) adalah ikan salmon dalam Siklus Fenian mitologi Irlandia yang memperoleh seluruh pengetahuan dunia dengan memakan sembilan buah hazel.",
    "en": "The Salmon of Knowledge (An Bradán Feasa) is a salmon of the Fenian Cycle of Irish myth that gained all the world's knowledge by eating nine hazelnuts.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "salmon-of-knowledge-c01",
      "salmon-of-knowledge-c02"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Salmon of Knowledge (Irlandia/Irish: An Bradán Feasa) adalah makhluk dalam Siklus Fenian (Fenian Cycle) mitologi Irlandia, kadang disamakan dengan peramal Fintan mac Bóchra, \"Sang Bijak\", yang pernah diubah menjadi salmon. Dalam The Boyhood Deeds of Fionn, seekor salmon biasa memakan sembilan buah hazel yang jatuh ke Well of Wisdom dari sembilan pohon di sekelilingnya, sehingga memperoleh seluruh pengetahuan dunia; orang pertama yang memakan dagingnya akan mendapat pengetahuan itu. Menurut artikel fr, buah hazel itu jatuh ke Sungai Boyne, kadang Shannon, dan salmon itu menjadi sangat besar.",
      "en": "The Salmon of Knowledge (Irish An Bradán Feasa) belongs to the Fenian Cycle of Irish myth and is sometimes identified with the seer Fintan mac Bóchra, 'the Wise', who was once turned into a salmon. In The Boyhood Deeds of Fionn, a plain salmon swallowed nine hazelnuts dropped into the Well of Wisdom by the nine trees round it, and so came to know everything in the world; whoever first ate its flesh would gain that knowledge. According to the fr article the nuts fell into the Boyne, or sometimes the Shannon, and the salmon grew enormous.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "salmon-of-knowledge-c01",
        "salmon-of-knowledge-c02",
        "salmon-of-knowledge-c06"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Penyair Finn Eces (Finegas) memancing salmon itu selama tujuh tahun, lalu menyerahkannya kepada pelayannya, Fionn putra Cumhall, untuk dimasak tanpa boleh dimakan. Saat memeriksa apakah ikan sudah matang, ibu jari Fionn terbakar setetes lemak panas, dan ia mengisapnya; seluruh kebijaksanaan salmon ternyata terkumpul di tetes itu. Finn Eces lalu memberikan sisa ikan kepadanya, dan sejak itu Fionn dapat memanggil pengetahuan itu cukup dengan menggigit ibu jarinya; kebijaksanaan inilah yang membuatnya menjadi pemimpin Fianna. Kisah serupa dalam Hanes Taliesin Wales menceritakan bagaimana penyair Taliesin memperoleh kebijaksanaannya.",
      "en": "The poet Finn Eces (Finegas) fished seven years for the salmon, then gave it to his servant Fionn, son of Cumhall, to cook but not to taste. Checking whether it was done, Fionn burnt his thumb on a drop of hot fat and sucked it; all of the salmon's wisdom had gathered in that drop. Finn Eces then let him eat the rest, and from then on Fionn could summon the knowledge just by biting his thumb; that wisdom made him leader of the Fianna. A similar tale in the Welsh Hanes Taliesin tells how the poet Taliesin won his wisdom.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "salmon-of-knowledge-c03",
        "salmon-of-knowledge-c04",
        "salmon-of-knowledge-c05"
      ]
    }
  ],

      "quote": "The Salmon of Knowledge (Irish: An Bradán Feasa ) is a creature in the Fenian Cycle of Irish mythology, sometimes identified with the mythical seer Fintan mac Bóchra who was known as \"The Wise\" and was once transformed into a salmon.",

    {
      "id": "salmon-of-knowledge-c02",
      "source_id": "salmon-of-knowledge-s1",
      "quote": "In the story, an ordinary salmon ate nine hazelnuts that fell into the Well of Wisdom (an Tobar Segais) from nine hazel trees that surrounded the well. By this act, the salmon gained all the world's knowledge. The first person to eat of its flesh would in turn gain this knowledge.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam The Boyhood Deeds of Fionn, salmon biasa memakan sembilan buah hazel yang jatuh ke Well of Wisdom dan memperoleh seluruh pengetahuan dunia.",

    {
      "id": "salmon-of-knowledge-c03",
      "source_id": "salmon-of-knowledge-s1",
      "quote": "The poet Finn Eces (or Finegas) spent seven years fishing for this salmon. Finally Finn caught the salmon and gave the fish to Fionn, his servant and son of Cumhall, with instructions to cook it but on no account eat any of it.",
      "locator": null,

      "source_id": "salmon-of-knowledge-s1",
      "quote": "Fionn ate the salmon and in so doing gained all the knowledge of the world. For the rest of his life, Fionn could draw upon this knowledge merely by biting his thumb. The deep knowledge and wisdom gained from the Salmon of Knowledge allowed Fionn to become the leader of the Fianna",

      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Fionn memperoleh seluruh pengetahuan, memanggilnya dengan menggigit ibu jari, dan menjadi pemimpin Fianna.",
        "en": "Fionn gained all knowledge, drew on it by biting his thumb, and became leader of the Fianna."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "salmon-of-knowledge-c05",
      "source_id": "salmon-of-knowledge-s1",
      "quote": "The Welsh Hanes Taliesin (16th c.) has a similar story of how the poet Taliesin received his wisdom, that also involves shape-shifting into the form of a fish.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Hanes Taliesin dari Wales (Welsh) memuat kisah serupa tentang Taliesin.",
        "en": "The Welsh Hanes Taliesin has a similar story about Taliesin."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "salmon-of-knowledge-c06",
      "source_id": "salmon-of-knowledge-s2",
      "quote": "est à l'origine un saumon ordinaire, qui mange les neuf noisettes tombés de l'arbre du savoir dans le fleuve Boyne — ou parfois le Shannon. Ce faisant, le saumon devient énorme et acquiert tout le savoir du monde, devenant plus sage que les hommes.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Saumon de la sagesse memakan sembilan buah hazel yang jatuh ke Sungai Boyne atau Shannon, menjadi sangat besar dan tahu segalanya.",
        "en": "The Saumon de la sagesse ate nine hazelnuts fallen into the Boyne or Shannon, grew enormous and knew everything."
      }
    }
  ],

  "traits": [],
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Sirona adalah dewi penyembuhan Kelt kuno yang dikaitkan dengan mata air penyembuh dan air panas, dipuja di Galia timur, provinsi Germania, dan provinsi Danube.",
    "en": "Sirona was an old Celtic healing goddess linked with curative and hot springs, honoured in eastern Gaul, the Germanies and along the Danube.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "sirona-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Sirona (juga Đirona atau Dirona) adalah dewi penyembuhan Kelt (Celtic) kuno yang dikaitkan dengan mata air penyembuh dan air panas, dipuja di Galia (Gaul) timur, provinsi Germania (Germanies), dan provinsi Danube. Ia terutama dikenal sebagai pasangan Apollo dengan julukan penyembuhnya Grannus, tetapi sejumlah persembahan menyebut namanya sendiri. Lambangnya yang paling khas adalah ular dan semangkuk telur, simbol penyembuhan dan pembaruan. Tak ada pengarang klasik yang menyebut namanya; bukti tentangnya hanya berupa prasasti dan gambar.",
      "en": "Sirona (also Đirona or Dirona) was an old Celtic goddess of healing linked with curative and hot springs and honoured in eastern Gaul, the Germanies and the Danube provinces. She is known mainly as Apollo's consort under his healing title Grannus, though some dedications name her by herself. Her most typical emblems are a snake and a bowl of eggs, signs of healing and renewal. No classical writer mentions her; what we know comes from inscriptions and images.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "sirona-c01",
        "sirona-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Kebanyakan sarjana menurunkan namanya dari kata Proto-Indo-Eropa untuk \"bintang\", sehingga namanya kira-kira berarti \"dewi bintang\". Pemujaannya berpusat di Germania dan Galia timur, terutama di lembah Moselle hulu di antara suku Treveri dan di sekitar Mainz, tetapi persembahan juga ditemukan di Raetia, Noricum, Pannonia, Dacia, dan kota Roma. Menurut artikel de, ia secara ikonografis disamakan dengan dewi penyembuh Yunani Hygieia, dikenal hanya dari prasasti persembahan tanpa mitologi yang diwariskan, dan tempat sucinya bersama Apollo dari abad ke-2 Masehi digali di Hochscheid (Hunsrück).",
      "en": "Most scholars take her name from the Proto-Indo-European word for 'star', giving roughly 'star goddess'. Her cult centred on the Germanies and eastern Gaul, especially the upper Moselle valley among the Treveri and around Mainz, but dedications also come from Raetia, Noricum, Pannonia, Dacia and the city of Rome. According to the de article she was iconographically equated with the Greek healing goddess Hygieia, is known only from votive inscriptions with no surviving myths, and a 2nd-century shrine she shared with Apollo was excavated at Hochscheid (Hunsrück).",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "sirona-c01",
      "source_id": "sirona-s1",
      "quote": "was an ancient Celtic goddess of healing, associated with curative and thermal springs, worshipped in eastern Gaul, the Germanies and the Danube provinces. She is attested chiefly as the consort of Apollo under his healing surname Grannus",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sirona (Đirona/Dirona) dewi penyembuhan Kelt (Celtic) kuno, mata air penyembuh, dipuja di Galia (Gaul) timur, Germania (Germanies), provinsi Danube; pasangan Apollo Grannus; di Prancis dan Jerman.",
        "en": "Sirona was an ancient Celtic healing goddess of curative springs in eastern Gaul, the Germanies and the Danube provinces; consort of Apollo Grannus."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sirona-c02",
      "source_id": "sirona-s1",
      "quote": "Her most distinctive attributes are the snake and a bowl of eggs, taken as emblems of healing and renewal. No classical author names her, the evidence being epigraphic and iconographic.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Lambangnya ular dan semangkuk telur; tak ada pengarang klasik yang menyebutnya.",
        "en": "Her emblems are a snake and a bowl of eggs; no classical author names her."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sirona-c03",
      "source_id": "sirona-s1",
      "quote": "Most scholars derive its first element from the Proto-Indo-European word for 'star', *h₂ster, which gives Proto-Celtic *sterā-, so that the name means something like 'star (goddess)'.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nama dari kata Proto-Indo-Eropa (Proto-Indo-European) untuk bintang (star).",
        "en": "The name derives from the Proto-Indo-European word for star."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sirona-c04",
      "source_id": "sirona-s1",
      "quote": "Her cult was concentrated in the Germanies and eastern Gaul, above all in the valley of the upper Moselle, among the Treveri, and in the Mainz area. Dedications also come from the territories of the Aedui and the Leuci, and from further afield: the Danube provinces of Raetia, Noricum and Pannonia, the province of Dacia, and the city of Rome.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pemujaan di Germania dan Galia timur, lembah Moselle hulu (Treveri), Mainz; juga Raetia, Noricum, Pannonia, Dacia, Roma (Rome).",
        "en": "Cult in the Germanies and eastern Gaul, upper Moselle (Treveri), Mainz; also Raetia, Noricum, Pannonia, Dacia and Rome."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sirona-c05",
      "source_id": "sirona-s1",
      "quote": "At Corseul in Brittany she is named Tsirona and is invoked together with the numen of the emperor, a pairing that points to her high standing.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di Corseul (Brittany) ia disebut Tsirona.",
        "en": "At Corseul in Brittany she is named Tsirona."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sirona-c06",
      "source_id": "sirona-s2",
      "quote": "Sirona, auch Đirona, war eine keltische Göttin der Heilung. Sie wurde ikonographisch der griechischen Heilgöttin Hygieia gleichgesetzt. Sie ist häufig Kultgenossin des Apollo Grannus.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sirona (Đirona) dewi penyembuhan Kelt (keltische Göttin der Heilung), disamakan dengan dewi Yunani (griechischen) Hygieia, rekan pemujaan Apollo Grannus.",
        "en": "Sirona (Đirona) was a Celtic goddess of healing (keltische Göttin der Heilung), equated with the Greek (griechischen) Hygieia, cult partner of Apollo Grannus."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sirona-c07",
      "source_id": "sirona-s2",
      "quote": "Sirona ist ausschließlich aus antiken Weiheinschriften bekannt, eine Mythologie ist nicht überliefert.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sirona hanya dikenal dari prasasti persembahan kuno; tak ada mitologi yang diwariskan.",
        "en": "Sirona is known only from ancient votive inscriptions; no mythology survives."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sirona-c08",
      "source_id": "sirona-s2",
      "quote": "Ein Heiligtum des Apollo und der Sirona des 2. Jahrhunderts n. Chr. wurde in Hochscheid (Hunsrück) ausgegraben.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {

        "en": "A 2nd-century AD shrine of Apollo and Sirona was excavated at Hochscheid (Hunsrück)."

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Brân the Blessed (Bendigeidfran, \"Gagak yang Diberkati\") adalah raksasa dan raja Britania dalam mitologi Wales, yang kepalanya tetap berbicara setelah dipenggal.",
    "en": "In Welsh myth Brân the Blessed (Bendigeidfran, 'Blessed Crow') is a giant who reigned over Britain, and his cut-off head kept on talking.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "bran-the-blessed-c01",
      "bran-the-blessed-c05"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Brân the Blessed (Wales/Welsh: Bendigeidfran atau Brân Fendigaidd, harfiah \"Gagak yang Diberkati\") adalah raksasa dan raja Britania (Britain) dalam mitologi Wales. Ia muncul dalam beberapa Welsh Triads, tetapi perannya terpenting ada di Cabang Kedua Mabinogi, Branwen ferch Llŷr. Ia putra Llŷr dan Penarddun serta saudara Brânwen, Manawydan, Nisien, dan Efnysien. Nama Brân biasanya diartikan gagak; ia dianggap dewa laut sekaligus dewa puisi dan para bard.",
      "en": "Brân the Blessed (Welsh Bendigeidfran or Brân Fendigaidd, 'Blessed Crow') is a giant who ruled Britain in Welsh myth. Several Welsh Triads mention him, though his main part is in the Second Branch of the Mabinogi, Branwen ferch Llŷr. Llŷr and Penarddun were his parents, and Brânwen, Manawydan, Nisien and Efnysien his siblings. Brân is usually rendered crow or raven; he is held to be a sea god and also a god of poetry and of bards.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "bran-the-blessed-c01",
        "bran-the-blessed-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Dalam Mabinogion, ia memberi raja Irlandia (Irish) Matholwch sebuah kuali ajaib yang dapat menghidupkan orang mati sebagai ganti rugi atas penghinaan Efnysien. Ketika Branwen dianiaya di Irlandia, Brân mengarungi Laut Irlandia (Irish Sea) untuk menyelamatkannya. Setelah perang yang hanya menyisakan tujuh orang, Brân yang terluka parah menyuruh mereka memenggal kepalanya dan membawanya pulang ke Britania; kepala itu terus berbicara dan menghibur mereka selama tujuh tahun di Harlech. Akhirnya kepala itu dikubur di Gwynfryn, \"Bukit Putih\" (diduga lokasi Tower of London), menghadap Prancis (France) untuk menangkal serbuan. Menurut artikel de, nama Bran sering menjadi sinonim untuk \"pejuang\" (Krieger).",
      "en": "In the Mabinogion he gives the Irish king Matholwch a magic cauldron able to revive the dead, as amends for Efnysien's insult. When Branwen is ill-treated in Ireland, Brân wades the Irish Sea to save her. After a war that leaves just seven men alive, the dying Brân tells them to cut off his head and carry it back to Britain; the head goes on talking and entertains them for seven years at Harlech. It is finally buried at the Gwynfryn, the 'White Hill' (thought to be the site of the Tower of London), facing France to ward off invasion. According to the de article the name Bran is often a synonym for 'warrior' (Krieger).",
      "claim_ids": [
        "bran-the-blessed-c03",
        "bran-the-blessed-c04",
        "bran-the-blessed-c05",
        "bran-the-blessed-c06",
        "bran-the-blessed-c08"
      ]
    }
  ],

      "id": "bran-the-blessed-c01",
      "source_id": "bran-the-blessed-s1",
      "quote": "Brân the Blessed (Welsh: Bendigeidfran or Brân Fendigaidd, literally \"Blessed Crow\") is a giant and king of Britain in Welsh mythology. He appears in several of the Welsh Triads, but his most significant role is in the Second Branch of the Mabinogi, Branwen ferch Llŷr. He is a son of Llŷr and Penarddun, and the brother of Brânwen, Manawydan, Nisien and Efnysien.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Brân the Blessed (Wales, Welsh: Bendigeidfran/Brân Fendigaidd, Gagak yang Diberkati) raksasa dan raja Britania (Britain), dalam Welsh Triads dan Cabang Kedua (Second Branch) Mabinogi, Branwen ferch Llŷr; putra Llŷr dan Penarddun, saudara Brânwen (Branwen), Manawydan, Nisien, Efnysien.",
        "en": "Brân the Blessed (Welsh Bendigeidfran/Brân Fendigaidd, Blessed Crow) is a giant king of Britain in the Welsh Triads and the Second Branch of the Mabinogi, Branwen ferch Llŷr; son of Llŷr and Penarddun, brother of Brânwen, Manawydan, Nisien, Efnysien."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "bran-the-blessed-c02",
      "source_id": "bran-the-blessed-s1",
      "quote": "The name \"Brân\" in Welsh is usually translated as crow or raven. He is considered a sea-god as well as a god of poetry and bards.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Brân berarti gagak; ia dianggap dewa laut serta dewa puisi dan bard.",
        "en": "Brân means crow or raven; he is considered a sea god and god of poetry and bards."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "bran-the-blessed-c03",
      "source_id": "bran-the-blessed-s1",
      "quote": "Matholwch is deeply offended until Brân offers him compensation in the form of a magic cauldron that can restore the dead to life.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam Mabinogion, Brân memberi Matholwch kuali ajaib penghidup orang mati; penghinaan oleh Efnysien; Irlandia (Irish).",
        "en": "In the Mabinogion, Brân gives Matholwch a magic cauldron that revives the dead after Efnysien's insult."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "bran-the-blessed-c04",
      "source_id": "bran-the-blessed-s1",
      "quote": "Brân wades across the Irish Sea to rescue her with his brother Manawydan and a huge host of warriors",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Brân mengarungi Laut Irlandia (Irish Sea) untuk menyelamatkan Branwen. (Ireland)",
        "en": "Brân wades across the Irish Sea to rescue Branwen. (Ireland)"
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "bran-the-blessed-c05",
      "source_id": "bran-the-blessed-s1",
      "quote": "The survivors are told by a mortally wounded Brân to cut off his head and to return it to Britain. For seven years the seven survivors stay in Harlech, where they are entertained by Brân's head, which continues to speak.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Brân menyuruh memenggal kepalanya dan membawanya ke Britania; kepala itu berbicara selama tujuh tahun di Harlech.",
        "en": "Brân has his head cut off and taken to Britain; it speaks for seven years at Harlech."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "bran-the-blessed-c06",
      "source_id": "bran-the-blessed-s1",
      "quote": "they take the now silent head to the Gwynfryn, the \"White Hill\" (thought to be the location where the Tower of London now stands), where they bury it facing France so as to ward off invasion.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kepala dikubur di Gwynfryn, Bukit Putih (White Hill, diduga Tower of London), menghadap Prancis (France).",
        "en": "The head is buried at the Gwynfryn, the White Hill (perhaps the Tower of London), facing France."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "bran-the-blessed-c07",
      "source_id": "bran-the-blessed-s2",
      "quote": "ist ein Riese aus den walisischen Sagen. Aus dem Walisischen übersetzt bedeutet Bran „Krähe“, oft wird er jedoch auch „Rabe“ genannt.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Bran der Gesegnete raksasa (Riese) dari legenda Wales (walisischen Sagen); Bran berarti gagak (Krähe) atau raven (Rabe).",
        "en": "Bran der Gesegnete is a giant (Riese) of Welsh legend (walisischen Sagen); Bran means crow (Krähe) or raven (Rabe)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "bran-the-blessed-c08",
      "source_id": "bran-the-blessed-s2",
      "quote": "Beides ist häufig ein Synonym für „Krieger“.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Gagak dan raven sering menjadi sinonim untuk pejuang (Krieger).",
        "en": "Crow and raven are often synonyms for warrior (Krieger)."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Cat-sìth adalah makhluk peri mitologi Kelt yang berwujud kucing hitam besar dengan bercak putih di dada, dikatakan menghantui Dataran Tinggi Skotlandia.",
    "en": "The cat-sìth is a fairy creature of Celtic myth, a big black cat with a white spot on its chest, said to haunt the Scottish Highlands.",
    "claim_ids": [
