    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Cat-sìth (Gaelik Skotlandia/Scottish Gaelic, jamak cait-shìth; Irlandia/Irish cat sí) adalah makhluk peri dari mitologi Kelt (Celtic), berupa kucing hitam besar dengan bercak putih di dada yang berjalan dengan kaki belakangnya, dan dikatakan menghantui Dataran Tinggi Skotlandia (Scottish Highlands). Legendanya lebih umum di Skotlandia (Scottish) daripada di Irlandia. Sebagian kepercayaan menganggapnya bukan peri, melainkan penyihir yang bisa berubah menjadi kucing sembilan kali. Ia sebesar anjing, dan di dekat manusia melengkungkan punggung serta menegakkan bulunya agar tampak lebih besar.",
      "en": "The cat-sìth (Scottish Gaelic, plural cait-shìth; Irish cat sí) is a fairy being of Celtic myth: a large black cat with a white chest spot that walks upright on its hind legs and is said to haunt the Scottish Highlands. Its tales are commoner in Scotland than in Ireland. Some folk belief held it to be not a fairy but a witch able to take cat form nine times. It is the size of a dog, and near people it arches its back and raises its fur to look bigger.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "cat-sith-c01",
        "cat-sith-c02",
        "cat-sith-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Cat-sìth diyakini mencuri jiwa orang yang baru meninggal dengan melangkahi jenazahnya, sehingga orang berjaga dalam vigili yang disebut Late Wake atau Fèill Fhadalach dan mengalihkan perhatiannya dengan musik, permainan, tarian, atau catnip. Sekitar Samhain orang meninggalkan sepiring susu untuknya; rumah yang melakukannya mendapat keberuntungan setahun, sedangkan yang tidak, susu sapinya dikeringkan. Menurut artikel de, cat-sìth biasanya tidak berniat mencelakai manusia, tetapi bisa menjadi sangat buas dan berbahaya bila dikejutkan sendirian di tempat terpencil atau diancam; legendanya diduga berasal dari pengamatan kucing Kellas.",
      "en": "The cat-sìth was thought to steal the souls of the newly dead by passing over the body, so people kept a vigil called the Late Wake or Fèill Fhadalach and distracted it with music, games, dancing or catnip. Around Samhain a saucer of milk was set out for it; houses that did so had a year's good luck, while those that didn't found their cows' milk dried up. According to the de article it usually means people no harm but can turn wild and dangerous if surprised alone in a remote place or threatened; its legend is thought to come from sightings of the Kellas cat.",

    {
      "id": "cat-sith-c01",
      "source_id": "cat-sith-s1",
      "quote": "is a fairy creature from Celtic mythology, said to resemble a large black cat with a white spot on its chest that walks on its hind legs. Legend has it that the spectral cat haunts the Scottish Highlands. The legends surrounding this creature are more common in Scottish folklore, but a few occur in Irish.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Cat-sìth (Gaelik Skotlandia, Scottish Gaelic; Irlandia, Irish cat sí) peri mitologi Kelt (Celtic), kucing hitam besar berbercak putih, berjalan dengan kaki belakang, di Dataran Tinggi Skotlandia (Scottish Highlands); di Inggris Raya dan Irlandia. Lebih umum di Skotlandia (Scotland) daripada Irlandia (Ireland).",
        "en": "The cat-sìth (Scottish Gaelic; Irish cat sí) is a fairy of Celtic myth, a large black cat with a white spot walking on its hind legs, in the Scottish Highlands. More common in Scotland than Ireland."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "cat-sith-c02",
      "source_id": "cat-sith-s1",
      "quote": "Some common folklore suggested that the cat-sìth was not a fairy, but a witch that could transform into a cat nine times.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebagian menganggapnya penyihir yang bisa menjadi kucing sembilan kali.",
        "en": "Some held it a witch able to become a cat nine times."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "cat-sith-c03",
      "source_id": "cat-sith-s1",
      "quote": "It is described as being as large as a dog and chooses to display itself with its back arched and bristles erect.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebesar anjing, punggung melengkung dan bulu tegak; mungkin terinspirasi kucing Kellas.",
        "en": "As large as a dog, back arched and bristles erect."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "cat-sith-c04",
      "source_id": "cat-sith-s1",
      "quote": "The cat-sìth were believed to steal the souls of the recently deceased that hadn't gone to the land of the dead. This would happen by a cat-sìth crossing over a new dead body.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Cat-sìth diyakini mencuri jiwa orang baru meninggal dengan melangkahi jenazah; dijaga dalam Late Wake (Fèill Fhadalach).",
        "en": "The cat-sìth was believed to steal souls of the newly dead by crossing over the body; guarded in the Late Wake (Fèill Fhadalach)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "cat-sith-c05",
      "source_id": "cat-sith-s1",
      "quote": "Around Samhain, it was custom to leave out a saucer of milk for them. If you did, your household would receive good luck for the year. If you didn't, the cat-sìth would return the favor and dry up the milk of the house's cows.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Saat Samhain diberi susu untuk keberuntungan; jika tidak, susu sapi dikeringkan; di Skotlandia.",
        "en": "At Samhain milk was left out for luck; otherwise it dried up the cows' milk."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "cat-sith-c06",
      "source_id": "cat-sith-s1",
      "quote": "To avoid the soul being taken during this period, people would stay to watch the body in a vigil called the Late Wake or Fèill Fhadalach in Irish Gaelic.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Orang berjaga dalam vigili Late Wake atau Fèill Fhadalach (Irish Gaelic).",
        "en": "People kept a vigil called the Late Wake or Fèill Fhadalach (Irish Gaelic)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "cat-sith-c07",
      "source_id": "cat-sith-s2",
      "quote": "ist eine Sagengestalt der keltischen Mythologie. Es handelt sich um eine große schwarze Katze, die in den Highlands leben soll.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Cait Sith tokoh mitologi Kelt (keltischen Mythologie), kucing hitam besar di Highlands; terkait Cu Sith (Cù-sìth).",
        "en": "Cait Sith is a figure of Celtic myth (keltischen Mythologie), a large black cat in the Highlands; linked to the Cu Sith (Cù-sìth)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "cat-sith-c08",
      "source_id": "cat-sith-s2",
      "quote": "Die Cait Sith ist üblicherweise nicht darauf aus, dem Menschen zu schaden. Jedoch, sollte man sie alleine in abgelegenen Orten überraschen oder sie bedrohen, dann kann sie sehr wild und gefährlich werden.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Cait Sith biasanya tidak mencelakai, tetapi bisa buas bila dikejutkan atau diancam; nama berarti kucing peri (Feenkatze).",
        "en": "Cait Sith usually means no harm but can turn wild if surprised or threatened; name means fairy cat (Feenkatze)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "cat-sith-c09",
      "source_id": "cat-sith-s2",
      "quote": "Es wird vermutet, dass die Sage von Beobachtungen der Kellas-Katze herrührt.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sagenya diduga dari pengamatan kucing Kellas (Kellas-Katze, Kellas cat).",
        "en": "The legend is thought to come from sightings of the Kellas cat (Kellas-Katze)."
      }
    }
  ],

    "id": "Clíodhna adalah Ratu Banshee dari Tuatha Dé Danann dalam mitologi Irlandia, dalam sebagian mitos dewi cinta dan kecantikan serta pelindung County Cork.",
    "en": "Clíodhna is a Queen of the Banshees of the Tuatha Dé Danann in Irish myth, in some myths a goddess of love and beauty and patron of County Cork.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "cliodhna-c01",
      "cliodhna-c02"

  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam mitologi Irlandia (Irish), Clíodhna (Clídna, Clíodna, Clíona, kadang dianglisisasi menjadi Cleena) adalah Ratu Banshee (Queen of the Banshees) dari Tuatha Dé Danann. Clíodna dari Carrigcleena adalah banshee kuat yang memerintah sióga (peri) di South Munster atau Desmond. Dalam sebagian mitos ia dewi cinta dan kecantikan serta pelindung County Cork. Ia dikatakan memiliki tiga burung berwarna cerah yang memakan apel dari pohon dunia lain dan nyanyiannya menyembuhkan penyakit. Istananya berada di tengah tumpukan batu sekitar 8 km dari Mallow, yang masih dikenal sebagai Carrig-Cleena, dan petani Munster menganggapnya pelindung mereka.",
      "en": "In Irish myth Clíodhna (Clídna, Clíodna, Clíona, sometimes anglicised Cleena) is a Queen of the Banshees among the Tuatha Dé Danann. As Clíodna of Carrigcleena she is a powerful banshee reigning over the sióga (fairies) of South Munster, or Desmond. Some myths make her a goddess of love and beauty and patron of County Cork. She is said to keep three bright-coloured birds that feed on apples from an otherworld tree and whose song cures sickness. Her palace lay inside a heap of rocks about 8 km from Mallow, still called Carrig-Cleena, and the Munster country folk looked on her as their benefactor.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "cliodhna-c01",
        "cliodhna-c02",
        "cliodhna-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Menurut legenda Cleena's Wave dalam Acallam na Senórach, Ciabham putra raja Ulster diselamatkan ke Tír Tairngire, negeri Manannan; putri druid Gebann, Cleena, jatuh cinta dan pergi bersamanya, tetapi di pantai Irlandia orang-orang Manannan membuatnya tertidur dengan musik dan sebuah ombak besar menyapunya. Tempat itu disebut Tonn Chlíodhna, \"Ombak Clíodhna\", dekat pelabuhan Glandore. Menurut artikel de, Cormac Laidir Mac Carthy, pembangun Blarney Castle, meminta bantuan dewi itu dalam sengketa hukum; ia disuruh mencium batu pertama yang dilihatnya pagi itu, lalu memenangkan perkara berkat kefasihannya dan memasang batu itu di dinding bentengnya.",
      "en": "In the legend of Cleena's Wave in the Acallam na Senórach, Ciabham son of the king of Ulster is rescued to Tír Tairngire, Manannan's land; the druid Gebann's daughter Cleena falls in love and leaves with him, but on the Irish shore Manannan's people lull her to sleep with music and a great wave sweeps her off. The spot was named Tonn Chlíodhna, 'Clíodhna's Wave', near Glandore harbour. According to the de article, Cormac Laidir Mac Carthy, builder of Blarney Castle, asked the goddess for help in a lawsuit; told to kiss the first stone he saw that morning, he won through eloquence and set the stone high in his castle walls.",

      ]

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "cliodhna-c01",
      "source_id": "cliodhna-s1",
      "quote": "In Irish mythology, Clíodhna (Clídna, Clíodna, Clíona, sometimes anglicised to Cleena) is a Queen of the Banshees of the Tuatha Dé Danann. Clíodna of Carrigcleena is the potent banshee that rules as queen over the sióga (fairies) of South Munster, or Desmond.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam mitologi Irlandia (Irish), Clíodhna (Clídna, Clíodna, Clíona, Cleena) Ratu Banshee (Queen of the Banshees) Tuatha Dé Danann; Clíodna dari Carrigcleena memerintah sióga di South Munster (Desmond); di Irlandia.",
        "en": "In Irish mythology Clíodhna (Clídna, Clíodna, Clíona, Cleena) is a Queen of the Banshees of the Tuatha Dé Danann; Clíodna of Carrigcleena rules the sióga of South Munster (Desmond)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "cliodhna-c02",
      "source_id": "cliodhna-s1",
      "quote": "In some Irish myths, Clíodhna is a goddess of love and beauty, and the patron of County Cork. She is said to have three brightly coloured birds who eat apples from an otherworldly tree and whose sweet song heals sickness.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam sebagian mitos ia dewi cinta dan kecantikan, pelindung County Cork, dengan tiga burung yang nyanyiannya menyembuhkan.",
        "en": "In some myths she is a goddess of love and beauty, patron of County Cork, with three birds whose song heals."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "cliodhna-c03",
      "source_id": "cliodhna-s1",
      "quote": "She had her palace in the heart of a pile of rocks, 5 mi (8.0 km) from Mallow, which is still commonly known by the name of Carrig-Cleena, and numerous legends about her are told among the Munster peasantry who considered her their benefactor.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Istananya di tumpukan batu dekat Mallow, Carrig-Cleena; petani Munster menganggapnya pelindung.",
        "en": "Her palace was in rocks near Mallow, Carrig-Cleena; Munster peasants saw her as benefactor."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "cliodhna-c04",
      "source_id": "cliodhna-s1",
      "quote": "This legend is recorded in Acallam na Senórach (\"Colloquy of the Ancients\"), longest-surviving work of medieval Irish literature. During the time of the Fianna lived Ciabham of the Curling Hair, son of the King of Ulster.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Legenda Cleena's Wave tercatat dalam Acallam na Senórach; Ciabham putra raja Ulster (King of Ulster).",
        "en": "The legend of Cleena's Wave is in the Acallam na Senórach; Ciabham was son of the King of Ulster."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "cliodhna-c05",
      "source_id": "cliodhna-s1",
      "quote": "They then reached Tír Tairngire (\"the land of promise\"), an otherworldly land ruled by Manannan their king. Ciabham is entertained at a feast within Manannan's city. The chief druid Gebann had a daughter named Cleena of the Fair Hair.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mereka tiba di Tír Tairngire, negeri Manannan; putri druid Gebann bernama Cleena.",
        "en": "They reached Tír Tairngire, Manannan's land; the druid Gebann's daughter was Cleena."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "cliodhna-c06",
      "source_id": "cliodhna-s1",
      "quote": "One of them treacherously played music which caused Cleena to fall asleep. Then a great wave came and swept her away. The place was then called Tonn Chlíodhna, \"Clíodhna's Wave\", which is near Glandore harbour.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Musik membuat Cleena tertidur, ombak besar menyapunya; tempat itu Tonn Chlíodhna, Ombak Clíodhna (Clíodhna's Wave), dekat Glandore; di pantai Irlandia (Irish).",
        "en": "Music lulled Cleena asleep and a great wave swept her away; the spot is Tonn Chlíodhna, Clíodhna's Wave, near Glandore."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "cliodhna-c07",
      "source_id": "cliodhna-s2",
      "quote": "Cliodhna wird als Banshee, als Túatha Dé Danann und als Gestalt aus der Keltischen Anderswelt gesehen.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Cliodhna dipandang sebagai Banshee, Túatha Dé Danann, dan sosok Dunia Lain Kelt (Keltischen Anderswelt).",
        "en": "Cliodhna is seen as a Banshee, one of the Túatha Dé Danann, and a figure of the Celtic Otherworld (Keltischen Anderswelt)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "cliodhna-c08",
      "source_id": "cliodhna-s2",
      "quote": "Einer anderen Legende nach rief Cormac Laidir Mac Carthy, der Erbauer des Blarney Castle, die Göttin um Hilfe bei einem Rechtsstreit an. Die wies ihn an, den ersten Stein, den er morgens sah, zu küssen.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Cormac Laidir Mac Carthy, pembangun Blarney Castle, meminta bantuan dewi (Göttin) itu dan disuruh mencium batu pertama yang dilihatnya.",
        "en": "Cormac Laidir Mac Carthy, builder of Blarney Castle, asked the goddess (Göttin) for help and was told to kiss the first stone he saw."
      }
    }
  ],

  "disposition": null,
  "traits": [],
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Coventina adalah dewi Romawi-Britania untuk sumur dan mata air, dikenal dari prasasti di sebuah mata air dekat Carrawburgh di Tembok Hadrianus.",
    "en": "Coventina was a Romano-British goddess of wells and springs, known from inscriptions at a spring near Carrawburgh on Hadrian's Wall.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "coventina-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Coventina adalah dewi Romawi-Britania (Romano-British) untuk sumur dan mata air. Ia dikenal dari banyak prasasti di satu situs di Northumberland, Inggris (England), sekitar mata air dekat Carrawburgh di Tembok Hadrianus (Hadrian's Wall). Mungkin ada prasasti lain, dua dari Hispania dan satu dari Narbonensis, yang merujuk kepadanya, tetapi ini diperdebatkan. Menurut artikel de, ia dewi penyembuhan dan mata air (Heil- und Quellgöttin) dalam mitologi Britania yang dipuja di Tembok Hadrianus (Hadrianswall), dan para legiuner (Legionäre) membangun tempat suci untuknya dekat benteng Brocolitia.",
      "en": "Coventina was a Romano-British deity of wells and springs. She is known from many inscriptions at a single site in Northumberland, England, around a spring near Carrawburgh on Hadrian's Wall. Other inscriptions, two from Hispania and one from Narbonensis, may also name her, but that is disputed. According to the de article she was a healing and spring goddess (Heil- und Quellgöttin) of British myth worshipped on Hadrian's Wall (Hadrianswall), and legionaries (Legionäre) built her a shrine near the fort of Brocolitia.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "coventina-c01",
        "coventina-c06",
        "coventina-c07"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Pada 1879 persembahan untuk Coventina ditemukan di area berdinding yang menampung aliran mata air yang kini disebut Coventina's Well. Isi sumur itu antara lain 13.487 koin dari masa Mark Anthony hingga Gratian, relief tiga nimfa air, dua lempeng persembahan untuk Coventina, sepuluh altar untuk Coventina dan Minerva, serta berbagai benda persembahan. Bukti timbunan koin menunjukkan pemujaan berakhir cukup mendadak sekitar 388, mungkin terkait dekret anti-pagan Theodosius I. Beberapa altar menggambarkannya dalam rupa nimfa Romawi: berbaring, setengah berpakaian, dan dikaitkan dengan air; pada satu altar ia tampil dalam wujud tiga atau dengan dua pendamping.",
      "en": "In 1879 offerings to Coventina were found in a walled area built to hold the outflow of a spring now called Coventina's Well. Inside the well lay 13,487 coins spanning Mark Anthony to Gratian, a carving of three water nymphs, two dedication slabs to Coventina, ten altars to Coventina and Minerva, and many other votive objects. Coin-hoard evidence points to a rather sudden end around 388, perhaps linked to the anti-pagan edicts of Theodosius I. Some altars show her as a typical Roman nymph, reclining, half-clad and tied to water; on one she appears threefold or with two attendants.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "coventina-c03",
        "coventina-c04",
        "coventina-c05",
        "coventina-c07"
      ]
    }
  ],

      "quote": "Coventina was a Romano-British goddess of wells and springs. She is known from multiple inscriptions at one site in Northumberland, England, an area surrounding a wellspring near Carrawburgh on Hadrian's Wall. It is possible that other inscriptions, two from Hispania and one from Narbonensis, refer to Coventina, but this is disputed.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Coventina dewi Romawi-Britania (Romano-British) sumur dan mata air, dari prasasti di Northumberland, Inggris (England), dekat Carrawburgh di Tembok Hadrianus (Hadrian's Wall); mungkin juga di Hispania dan Narbonensis.",
        "en": "Coventina was a Romano-British goddess of wells and springs, known from inscriptions in Northumberland, England, near Carrawburgh on Hadrian's Wall; perhaps also Hispania and Narbonensis."

    {
      "id": "coventina-c02",
      "source_id": "coventina-s1",
      "quote": "In 1879, dedications to Coventina and votive deposits were found in a walled area which had been built to contain the outflow from a spring now called \"Coventina's Well\".",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tahun 1879 persembahan ditemukan di area berdinding mata air Coventina's Well.",
        "en": "In 1879 dedications were found in a walled area around the spring called Coventina's Well."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "coventina-c03",
      "source_id": "coventina-s1",
      "quote": "The contents of the well included 13,487 coins from Mark Anthony to Gratian, a relief of three water nymphs, the head of a male statue, two dedication slabs to the goddess Coventina, ten altars to Coventina and Minerva, two clay incense burners, and a wide range of votive objects.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Isi sumur: 13.487 koin dari Mark Anthony hingga Gratian, relief tiga nimfa, sepuluh altar untuk Coventina dan Minerva.",
        "en": "The well held 13,487 coins from Mark Anthony to Gratian, a relief of three nymphs, ten altars to Coventina and Minerva."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "coventina-c04",
      "source_id": "coventina-s1",
      "quote": "Evidence from coin hoards and stones which covered them and those also blocking the well suggest a fairly abrupt end around 388, perhaps due to events linked to anti-pagan edicts of Theodosius I.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pemujaan berakhir mendadak sekitar 388, mungkin karena dekret anti-pagan Theodosius I.",
        "en": "Worship ended abruptly around 388, perhaps due to Theodosius I's anti-pagan edicts."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "coventina-c05",
      "source_id": "coventina-s1",
      "quote": "Excavation of the site revealed several inscribed altars, some with depictions of Coventina in typical Roman nymph form: reclining, partially clothed and associated with water. On one, Coventina is either depicted in triple form or with two attendants.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Altar menggambarkan Coventina sebagai nimfa Romawi (Roman); satu dalam wujud tiga atau dengan dua pendamping.",
        "en": "Altars show Coventina as a Roman nymph; one in triple form or with two attendants."

        "en": "Coventina was a healing and spring goddess (Heil- und Quellgöttin) of British myth, worshipped at Hadrian's Wall (Hadrianswall)."
